# War against the Islamic State

This article is about the global war fought against the Islamic State. For the war fought by Iraq against the Islamic State, see [War in Iraq (2013–2017)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Iraq_\(2013%E2%80%932017\)). For the Islamic State's military involvement in Syria, see [Syrian civil war](30741795.md). For Egypt, see [Sinai insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_insurgency).

| War against the Islamic State |  |
| --- | --- |
| Part of the [post-Cold War era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-Cold_War_era), [war on terror](13425800.md), [Iraqi conflict](55579888.md), [Syrian civil war](30741795.md) and its [spillover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spillover_of_the_Syrian_Civil_War), [Afghan conflict](27576770.md), [Libyan crisis](44791215.md), [Yemeni crisis](45326059.md), [Somali Civil War](3070332.md), [Boko Haram insurgency](27868253.md), [insurgency in the Maghreb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurgency_in_the_Maghreb_\(2002-present\)) and [war in the Sahel](70938169.md), and the [insurgency in the North Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurgency_in_the_North_Caucasus) |  |
| **From top to bottom, left to right:** [(ISOF)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_Counter_Terrorism_Service) Humvee on the street of [Mosul](200475.md), Northern Iraq, 16 November 2016 during the [Battle of Mosul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Mosul_\(2016%E2%80%9317\)) A French [Dassault Rafale](33731380.md) lands on [USS Carl Vinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Carl_Vinson) during [Opération Chammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Op%C3%A9ration_Chammal) A pair of Russian Air Force [Tu-22M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tupolev_Tu-22M) bombers conducting a strike in Syria [U.S. Marines](17349325.md) conduct a fire mission in northern Syria A pair of U.S. Air Force [F-15E Strike Eagle](495724.md) fighters over Iraq Military situation in the former [IS territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territory_of_the_Islamic_State) in Iraq, Syria and Lebanon as of 31 January 2026  [Map of the current military situation in Iraq, Syria, and Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Syrian,_Iraqi,_and_Lebanese_insurgencies_detailed_map) [Map of the current military situation in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Libyan_Civil_War_detailed_map) [Map of the current military situation in Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Nigerian_insurgency_detailed_map) [Map of the current military situation in Sinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Sinai_insurgency_detailed_map) [Map of the current military situation in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Yemeni_Civil_War_detailed_map) |  |
| Date8 April 2013 – present (13 years, 5 months, 3 weeks and 4 days)Location[Iraq](7515928.md), [Syria](7515849.md), [Egypt](8087628.md), [Libya](17633.md), [Nigeria](21383.md), [Afghanistan](737.md), [Mozambique](19301.md), the [North Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Caucasus), and [Southeast Asia](28741.md)Status  Coalition victory (territorial, in Libya, Afghanistan, Egypt, Syria, Iraq and the Philippines) Ongoing medium-intensity insurgency  Airstrikes on IS positions in Iraq, Syria, [Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2015_Egyptian_airstrikes_in_Libya), Nigeria and Afghanistan Multinational humanitarian efforts Arming and supporting local ground forces Millions of civilians in Iraq and Syria flee their homes, sparking a refugee crisis Terrorist attacks in Paris ([Jan 2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2015_%C3%8Ele-de-France_attacks) and [Nov 2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2015_Paris_attacks)), Brussels ([Mar 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Brussels_bombings)) [and many other places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_terrorist_incidents_linked_to_the_Islamic_State) Thousands of civilians executed by IS forces in Iraq and Syria IS controlled around 40% of Iraq at its peak in mid-2014[^cite_note-1] IS controlled around 50% of Syria by late May 2015[^cite_note-2][^cite_note-3] Emergence of independently-governed Kurdish regions IS military defeated and lost all of its territory in Libya in December 2017[^cite_note-4][^cite_note-moves_to_desert_valleys-5] Boko Haram loses territory, but [its insurgency](27868253.md) continues[^cite_note-6] IS controlled 5.67% of Syria's land by November 2017[^cite_note-ISIS_now_controls_less_than_8%_of_Syrian_territory_–_Russian_General_Staff-7] and around 3% of Iraq by October 2017[^cite_note-8] IS loses all territory in Iraq and most territory in Syria in December 2017[^cite_note-\:14-9] IS loses all remaining territory in Syria in March 2019[^cite_note-10] |  |

Many states began to intervene against the [Islamic State](9087364.md) (IS), in both the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md) and the [War in Iraq (2013–2017)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Iraq_\(2013%E2%80%932017\)), in response to its rapid territorial gains from its [2014 invasion of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_invasion_of_Iraq), widely condemned [executions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_captives_by_ISIL), [human rights abuses](9087364.md#Human_rights_abuse_and_war_crime_findings) and the fear of further [spillovers of the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spillover_of_the_Syrian_civil_war). In later years, there were also minor interventions by some states against IS-affiliated groups in [Nigeria](21383.md) and [Libya](17633.md). All these efforts significantly degraded the Islamic State's capabilities by around 2019–2020. While moderate fighting continues in Syria, as of 2026, IS has been contained to a small area and force capability.

In mid-June 2014, [Iran](14653.md), according to American and British information, started flying drones over [Iraq](7515928.md), and Iranian soldiers were in Iraq fighting IS. Simultaneously, the US ordered a small number of troops to Iraq and started flying crewed aircraft over Iraq. In July 2014, Iran sent [Sukhoi Su-25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukhoi_Su-25) aircraft to Iraq, and [Hezbollah](13919.md) purportedly sent trainers and advisers to Iraq in order to help Shia [militias](45671374.md) to monitor IS's movements. In August 2014, the US and Iran separately began a campaign of airstrikes on IS targets in Iraq. Since then, fourteen countries in a [US-led coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US-led_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%932021\)) have also executed airstrikes on IS in Iraq and in [Syria](7515849.md). Starting from September 2014, the US began closely co-operating with [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) and [Jordan](7515964.md) to wage a co-ordinated [aerial bombing campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerial_bombing) against IS targets across Iraq and Syria.[^cite_note-auto-39]

In September 2015, [Russian forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia%E2%80%93Syria%E2%80%93Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_coalition) launched their [military intervention in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_intervention_in_the_Syrian_civil_war) to support their ally, [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md), in the fight against the [Islamic State](9087364.md). Although Moscow officially portrayed its intervention as an anti-IS campaign and publicly declared support for the "patriotic [Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition)", the vast majority of its bombings were focused on destroying bases of the Syrian opposition militias of the [Free Syrian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Syrian_Army) (FSA) and [Southern Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Front_\(Syrian_rebel_group\)).[^cite_note-137] On the other hand, the United States and its Western allies have been opposed to the former [Ba'athist regime](57134800.md) for its purported [state-sponsorship of terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_Bashar_al-Assad#State-sponsorship_of_terrorism), violent repression of the [Syrian revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Syrian_Revolution) and extensive [use of chemical weapons](39668667.md). The [US-led coalition](44618350.md) trained, equipped and supported secular Free Syrian and [Kurdish militias](48205248.md) opposed to the Assad government during its anti-IS campaign.[^cite_note-138] In the months following the beginning of both air campaigns, IS began to lose ground in both Iraq and [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-139] Civilian deaths from airstrikes began to mount in 2015 and 2016.[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141] In mid-2016, the US and Russia planned to begin coordinating their airstrikes; however, this coordination did not materialize.[^cite_note-142][^cite_note-143]

As of December 2017, IS was estimated to control no territory in Iraq, and 5% of Syrian territory, after prolonged actions.[^cite_note-144] On 9 December 2017, Iraq declared victory in the fight against ISIL and stated that the War in Iraq was over.[^cite_note-\:14-9][^cite_note-145] On 23 March 2019, IS was defeated territorially in Syria after losing the [Battle of Baghuz Fawqani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Baghuz_Fawqani), after which the group was forced into an [insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Syria_insurgency).[^cite_note-146] IS's leader, [Abu Bakr al-Baghdadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Bakr_al-Baghdadi), died during a US [special operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_special_operations_forces) [raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Abu_Bakr_al-Baghdadi) in northern Syria in October 2019[^cite_note-147] and was succeeded by [Abu Ibrahim al-Hashimi al-Qurashi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Ibrahim_al-Hashimi_al-Qurashi). In August 2020, over 10,000 IS fighters were estimated to remain in Syria and Iraq, mainly as [sleeper cells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sleeper_cell).[^cite_note-148]

## International coalitions against the Islamic State

### US-led coalitions

On the margins of the [4/5 September 2014 NATO summit in Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Wales_summit), on 5 September 2014, [U.S. Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Secretary_of_State) [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) invited Ministers of [Australia](4689264.md), [Canada](5042916.md), [Denmark](76972.md), [France](5843419.md), [Germany](11867.md), [Italy](14532.md), [Turkey](11125639.md) and the [United Kingdom](31717.md), for a separate meeting[^cite_note-nrc5Sep-149][^cite_note-nrc9Sep-150] in which he pressed them to support the fight against ISIL militarily and financially.[^cite_note-TIME5Sep-151] Those nine countries agreed to do so by supporting anti-ISIL forces in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md) with supplies and air support, according to a statement that day from Kerry and [U.S. Secretary of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Secretary_of_Defense) [Hagel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Hagel).[^cite_note-TIME5Sep-151] In September 2014, [Jordanian](377729.md) and [Saudi Air Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Air_Force) began their co-ordination with [United States](3434750.md) to wage a joint [aerial bombing campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerial_bombing) against IS bases in its [territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territory_of_the_Islamic_State) across Iraq and Syria.[^cite_note-auto-39][^cite_note-hp1-152]

Main article: [Operation Inherent Resolve](44120613.md)

On 17 October 2014, the [Department of Defense](7279897.md) formally established [Combined Joint Task Force – Operation Inherent Resolve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_Joint_Task_Force) (CJTF-OIR)—"in order to formalize ongoing military actions against the rising threat posed by ISIS in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md)."[^cite_note-153]

On 3 December 2014, at the [NATO headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_headquarters) in [Brussels](3708.md), diplomats[^cite_note-france24-4Dec-154] and foreign ministers[^cite_note-eeas5Dec-155] from 59 countries gathered to plot a way forward against the threat of ISIL.[^cite_note-france24-4Dec-154] [U.S. Secretary of State](32293.md) [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) told the gathering, that "defeating the ideology, the funding, the recruitment" of Daesh (ISIL) must be the primary focus of their discussion, more important than airstrikes and other military action.[^cite_note-france24-4Dec-154]

The countries represented on 3 December were: the nine countries of the above-mentioned 5 September coalition in [Wales](69894.md) (see above); the extra 18 countries of the 15 September [France](5843419.md)-led coalition in [Paris](22989.md) (see below) except for [China](5405.md) and [Russia](25391.md); and 33 additional countries: [Albania](738.md), [Austria](26964606.md), [Bosnia-Herzegovina](3463.md), [Bulgaria](3415.md), [Croatia](5573.md), [Cyprus](5593.md), [Estonia](28222445.md), [Finland](10577.md), [Georgia](48768.md), [Greece](12108.md), [Hungary](13275.md), [Israel](9282173.md), [Iceland](14531.md), [Ireland](14560.md), [Kosovo](17391.md), [Latvia](17514.md), [Lithuania](17675.md), [Luxembourg](17515.md), [Macedonia](23564616.md), [Moldova](19260.md), [Montenegro](20760.md), [Morocco](19291.md), [New Zealand](4913064.md), [Portugal](23033.md), [South Korea](27019.md), [Romania](25445.md), [Serbia](29265.md), [Singapore](27318.md), [Slovakia](26830.md), [Slovenia](27338.md), [Somalia](27358.md), [Sweden](5058739.md), [Taiwan](25734.md) and [Ukraine](31750.md).[^cite_note-USstate,3Dec-156]

They styled themselves as the *Global Coalition to Counter the Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant (ISIL)*, and agreed to a strategy that included:

- exposing ISIL's true nature;

- cutting off ISIL's financing and funding;

- supporting military operations.

  [^cite_note-USstate,3Dec-156]

### France-led coalition

Main article: [Opération Chammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Op%C3%A9ration_Chammal)

On 15 September 2014, at the 'International Conference on Peace and Security in [Iraq](7515928.md)' hosted by the [French President](24899.md) [François Hollande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande) in [Paris](22989.md), 26 countries were represented: the countries of a [US](3434750.md)-led coalition that on 5 September in [Wales](69894.md) (see above) had agreed on a coalition against ISIL except [Australia](4689264.md) and [Poland](22936.md), and furthermore [Bahrain](18933277.md), [Egypt](8087628.md), Iraq, [Jordan](7515964.md), [Kuwait](7515890.md), [Lebanon](17771.md), [Oman](22316.md), [Qatar](8238258.md), [Saudi Arabia](349303.md), [United Arab Emirates](69328.md), [Belgium](3343.md), [China](5405.md), [Czech Republic](5321.md), [Japan](15573.md), the [Netherlands](21148.md), [Norway](21241.md), [Russia](25391.md) and [Spain](26667.md).[^cite_note-FrDiplomatie-157] They committed themselves to supporting the Iraqi government with military assistance in its fight against ISIL, and they reaffirmed their commitment to [UNSC](31956.md) Resolution 2170 of 15 August (condemning all trade with ISIL and urging to prevent all financial donations and all payments of ransoms to ISIL),[^cite_note-unsc2170-158] so reported the [French government](3398091.md).[^cite_note-FrDiplomatie-157]

In retaliation for the [November 2015 Paris attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2015_Paris_attacks), the [French Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Air_Force) significantly intensified airstrikes against ISIL targets in [Syria](7515849.md), hitting among other targets the Syrian city of [Raqqa](858965.md), the de facto capital of ISIL. The [French Navy](383256.md) deployed the aircraft carrier [*Charles de Gaulle*](639127.md) with eighteen [Rafale](33731380.md), eight [Mirage 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Mirage_2000), two [E-2 Hawkeye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_Grumman_E-2_Hawkeye) and 4 helicopters. The aircraft carrier travelled with the [frigate](11464.md) [*Chevalier Paul*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_frigate_Chevalier_Paul) and [HMS *Kent*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Kent_\(F78\)).

### Russia-led coalition

Main article: [Russia–Syria–Iran–Iraq coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia%E2%80%93Syria%E2%80%93Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_coalition)

At the end of September 2015, [Russia](25391.md), [Iraq](7515928.md), [Iran](14653.md) and [Syria](7515849.md) set up a 'joint information center' in [Baghdad](4492.md) to gather, process and analyse intelligence regarding ISIL and operations near the Syria-Iraq border.[^cite_note-159] On 30 September 2015, Russia began its [air campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_military_intervention_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War) on the side and in support of the [Syrian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Syria).

[Russia](25391.md) was also reported to have reached agreements on co-ordination of operations in [Syria](7515849.md) with [Jordan](7515964.md) and [Israel](9282173.md).[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161]

On 14 March 2016, [Russian President](239646.md) [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) announced a partial withdrawal from Syrian territory, citing the success of the ongoing ceasefire and greater security of the [Syrian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Syria).[^cite_note-162]

On 10 December 2017 [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) ordered a similar withdrawal of Russian forces from [Syria](7515849.md), stating that a complete withdrawal would be dependent on the ongoing situation.[^cite_note-163]

### Muslim states' coalition

Main article: [Islamic Military Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Military_Alliance)

On 14 December 2015, Saudi Deputy Crown Prince and Defense Minister [Mohammed bin Salman Al Saud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_bin_Salman_Al_Saud) announced that 34 countries would join in the fight against [Muslim extremism](8734632.md), which he called a "disease." Based out of [Riyadh](51554.md), [Saudi Arabia](349303.md), the coalition includes [Bahrain](18933277.md), [Bangladesh](3454.md), [Benin](3459.md), [Chad](5488.md), [Comoros](5403.md), [Côte d'Ivoire](57744.md), [Djibouti](17207794.md), [Egypt](8087628.md), [Gabon](12027.md), [Guinea](12176.md), [Jordan](7515964.md), [Kuwait](7515890.md), [Lebanon](17771.md), [Libya](17633.md), [Maldives](19117.md), [Mali](19127.md), [Malaysia](3607937.md), [Morocco](19291.md), [Mauritania](18778516.md), [Niger](21373.md), [Nigeria](21383.md), [Pakistan](23235.md), [Palestine](241405.md), [Qatar](8238258.md), Saudi Arabia, [Senegal](27278.md), [Sierra Leone](27308.md), [Somalia](27358.md), [Sudan](27421.md), [Turkey](11125639.md), [Togo](30138.md), [Tunisia](30188.md), the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) and [Yemen](350939.md).[^cite_note-164]

## Syria

### US-led intervention in Syria

Main article: [US intervention in the Syrian civil war](43683196.md)



U.S. soldiers from 1st Battalion, [6th Infantry Regiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6th_Infantry_Regiment_\(United_States\)) near an oil facility in eastern Syria, 27 October 2020

#### Hostage rescue attempt

Main article: [2014 American rescue mission in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_American_rescue_mission_in_Syria)

On 4 July 2014, the [U.S](3434750.md). bombed the "Osama bin Laden" ISIL military base in the village of Uqayrishah, [Syria](7515849.md). Two dozen American [Delta Force](328809.md) operators then touched down in an effort to rescue hostages, including [James Foley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Foley_\(photojournalist\)). The effort failed, with the hostages having been moved to another location days prior.[^cite_note-telegraph-165][^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168] In [a series of videos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISIL_beheading_incidents), Foley, [Steven Joel Sotloff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Joel_Sotloff), and several more hostages were murdered.[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170]

#### Aerial surveillance

On 26 August 2014, the [U.S](3434750.md). began sending [surveillance flights](20338498.md), including [drones](58900.md), into [Syria](7515849.md) to gather intelligence. The Syrian Arab Republic was not asked for permission.[^cite_note-abcsyria-171][^cite_note-172] \
On 28 August, speaking about combating ISIL in [Syria](7515849.md), President Obama said "we don't have a strategy yet."[^cite_note-173]

The [British Royal Air Force](25679.md) has been operating over [Syria](7515849.md) in a surveillance role since 21 October 2014, making the [UK](31717.md) the first Western country other than the [United States](3434750.md) to operate in both [Iraq](7515928.md) and Syria simultaneously.[^cite_note-UKinSyria-174]

#### Arming and training rebels

Main articles: [Syrian Train and Equip Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Train_and_Equip_Program) and [Timber Sycamore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timber_Sycamore)



A U.S. [Green Beret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army_Special_Forces) demonstrates how to quickly fix a [firearm malfunction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firearm_malfunction) to [Revolutionary Commando Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary_Commando_Army) fighters at [al-Tanf garrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Tanf_\(U.S._military_base\)), 13 March 2020

At the direction of President Obama, the [U.S. Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) played an active role since the early stages of the [Syrian Civil War](30741795.md).[^cite_note-175][^cite_note-176] The [U.S](3434750.md). initially supplied the vetted militias of the [Free Syrian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Syrian_Army) with non-lethal aid but soon escalated to providing training, money, and intelligence to the rebel commanders.[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178][^cite_note-frontline-2014-05-26-179] In June 2014, Obama requested Congressional authorization of \$0.5 billion to train, arm and support vetted Free Syrian militias as a counter-force against both [Assad regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assad_regime) and [IS](9087364.md).[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181]

&#x20;On 17 September 2014, the [House of Representatives](19468510.md) voted to authorize the proposal to train and arm pro-Western [Free Syrian militias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Syrian_militias), with the objective of training 5,000–10,000 troops.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183][^cite_note-184] Syrian opposition's [National Revolutionary Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Coalition_of_Syrian_Revolutionary_and_Opposition_Forces) welcomed Obama's announcement of extending the anti-IS bombing campaign into Syria, stating:&#x20;

> "The Syrian Coalition ... stands ready and willing to partner with the international community not only to defeat ISIS but also rid the [Syrian people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_people) of the tyranny of the [Assad regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assad_regime)"[^cite_note-Roberts,_Ackerman-185]

The [United Kingdom](31717.md) announced in March 2015 that it would send 75 military personnel to help train Western-vetted [Free Syrian militias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Syrian_militias) in the use of small arms, infantry tactics and basic medical skills. The training was supposed to take place in [Turkey](11125639.md) as part of the [U.S](3434750.md).-led effort.[^cite_note-186]

According to the [United States Department of Defense](7279897.md), [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) proposed that they could provide training to [Syrian rebels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belligerents_in_the_Syrian_civil_war) so they could return to [Syria](7515849.md) and battle ISIL.[^cite_note-DoDoct8-187] The effort to train a large force of [Syrian rebels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belligerents_in_the_Syrian_civil_war) from anti-Assad factions to fight ISIL ultimately failed, with only 54 fighters in [Division 30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_30) trained and many captured, killed or not fighting.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190]

#### Multi-national airstrikes

See also: [Dutch involvement in the Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_involvement_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War), [Operation Okra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Okra), [Operation Impact § In Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Impact#In_Syria), [Opération Chammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Op%C3%A9ration_Chammal), [Jordanian intervention in the Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_intervention_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War), [Turkish involvement in the Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_involvement_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War), [Operation Shader § Intervention in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Shader#Intervention_in_Syria), and [German intervention against ISIL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_intervention_against_ISIL)



[U.S. Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_Strike_Group_Two) launching Tomahawk missiles from the [Persian Gulf](24761.md) and the [Red Sea](26191.md) against ISIL targets in Syria, 23 September 2014



[Peshmerga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshmerga) helicopter near the besieged city of [Sinjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinjar)

U.S. President Barack Obama announced on 10 September 2014 that he would begin to extend aerial campaign to [Syria](7515849.md) with or without congressional approval.[^cite_note-aje11sept-191] Many Senators were opposed to Obama's policy of unilateral intervention, without requesting a congressional mandate.[^cite_note-Ackerman-192] While Obama acknowledged the broad consensus in the [US intelligence community](501244.md) that IS were not a "current threat" to United States, he claimed that IS posed a future danger to the US, citing the [anti-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Americanism) rhetoric of IS leadership.[^cite_note-Roberts,_Ackerman-185] [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) leadership preferred a greater involvement of US boots to combat IS, but this was rejected by Obama, who instead favoured working with [Iraqi Ground forces](1576797.md), [Peshmerga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshmerga) and [Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition) militias.[^cite_note-Ackerman-192]

Starting on 22 September 2014, the [U.S](3434750.md)., [Bahrain](18933277.md), [Jordan](7515964.md), [Qatar](8238258.md), [Saudi Arabia](349303.md), and the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) began numerous large-scale airstrikes against ISIL targets in Syria[^cite_note-foxnewsarabpartners-193] with fighters, bombers, and sea-based [Tomahawk cruise missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomahawk_\(missile_family\)).[^cite_note-cnnsyriaairstrikes-194] The strikes were the largest aerial bombing operations launched against IS targets since US launched its military campaign against IS in August 2014.[^cite_note-Chulov,_Ackerman,_Lewis-195]

[Hadi al-Bahra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadi_al-Bahra), [President of the National Coalition of Syrian Revolutionary and Opposition Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Coalition_of_Syrian_Revolutionary_and_Opposition_Forces#Presidents), stated that the strikes were "necessary" to aid the [Syrian people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_people)'s fight against ISIL and urged the Coalition Forces to steer clear of civilian casualties.[^cite_note-Chulov,_Ackerman,_Lewis-195] United States has ruled out any co-operation with [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md) in the fight against IS, instead issuing warnings to the Syrian military to disengage from Coalition aircraft. [State Department spokesperson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Department_Spokesperson) Jen Psaki stated that no notifications had been issued to [Assad regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assad_regime) with regard to [US operations in Syrian airspace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American-led_intervention_in_the_Syrian_civil_war).[^cite_note-Chulov,_Ackerman,_Lewis-195]

Additionally, on the first night, the U.S. forces also launched eight cruise missile strikes against the [al-Qaeda](1921.md)-affiliated [Khorasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khorasan_\(Islamic_group\)).[^cite_note-196] Many airstrikes were focused against IS command & control facilities, training camps, supply depots, military facilities, etc. in and around [Raqqa](858965.md).[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-Chulov,_Ackerman,_Lewis-195] Strikes [continue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American-led_intervention_in_Syria#Air_campaign) to take place in Syria daily. In early November early December 2014, the U.S. launched additional airstrikes against the same group. In November 2014, [Morocco](19291.md) sent 3 [F-16s](11642.md) to be deployed in UAE, to fight ISIL in [Iraq](5043324.md) and Syria under U.S.-led operations.[^cite_note-NYTMO-198][^cite_note-WTMO-199][^cite_note-\:8-200]

On 24 December 2014, ISIL shot down a Jordanian fighter jet over [Syria](7515849.md) and captured its pilot, [Jordanian air force](377729.md) lieutenant [Muath Al-Kasasbeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muath_Al-Kasasbeh). Al-Kasabeh was offered in exchange for captured ISIL fighters. [Jordan](7515964.md) offered to make the exchange, but demanded "proof of life" first. However, Al-Kasabeh had already been executed by immolation. When video of the pilot's execution was released, a moratorium on executions in [Jordan](7515964.md) was lifted and the [Al-Qaida](1921.md) operatives, [Sajida al-Rishawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sajida_al-Rishawi) and [Ziad al-Karbouli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ziad_Khalaf_Raja_al-Karbouly) were executed.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202]

On 21 August 2015, three ISIL fighters, two with UK nationality, were targeted and killed in [Raqqa](858965.md), [Syria](7515849.md) by a [British Royal Air Force](25679.md) [MQ-9 Reaper](4769988.md) strike. Prime Minister David Cameron gave a statement to Parliament that one of the British nationals targeted had been plotting attacks in the [United Kingdom](31717.md). Another British national was killed in a separate air strike by US forces in Raqqa on 24 August.[^cite_note-\:9-203]



[Raqqa](858965.md) suffered extensive damage during the [Battle of Raqqa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Raqqa_\(2017\))

In October and November 2015, the [U.S](3434750.md). intensified its airstrikes on ISIL-held oil facilities in an operation named "[Tidal Wave II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Tidal_Wave_II)", after the [World War II](32927.md) [campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Tidal_Wave) against [Axis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis_powers) [oil targets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_Campaign_of_World_War_II) in [Romania](25445.md). The U.S. strategy aimed "to knock out specific installations for six months to a year" by focusing on facilities near [Deir el-Zour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_el-Zour). The Omar oil field, which produced 30,000 barrels of oil per day and \$1.7 million to \$5.1 million in revenue per month at full capacity, was hit on 21 October, reducing it to roughly a third of its capacity. French aircraft also participated in the strikes.[^cite_note-204]

On 16 November 2015, a U.S. Operation Tidal Wave II sortie destroyed 116 ISIL fuel tankers clustered near [Abu Kamal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Kamal), a city on the [Syrian border with Iraq](46975552.md). Four A-10 Thunderbolt IIs and two AC-130 Spectre gunships participated in the raid. Before attacking the trucks the planes conducted several low-level, 'show of force' passes.[^cite_note-205]

On 2 December 2015, the [Parliament of the United Kingdom](13964.md) voted in favour (397 to 223) to authorise air strikes in [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-206] Within hours, [RAF](25679.md) Tornado jets carried out their first air strikes, targeting the Omar oil fields in eastern Syria, which were under ISIL control.[^cite_note-207] Tornado GR-4 jets were used for surveillance and a further six Typhoons left [RAF Lossiemouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAF_Lossiemouth), [Scotland](26994.md) to join forces at [RAF Akrotiri](432784.md), [Cyprus](5593.md).[^cite_note-208]

On 4 December 2015 [Germany intervened](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_intervention_against_ISIL) in reaction to the [November 2015 Paris attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2015_Paris_attacks) by sending the frigate [*Augsburg* (F213)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_frigate_Augsburg_\(F213\)) and [Panavia Tornado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panavia_Tornado) reconnaissance aircraft to the region.[^cite_note-209] The *Augsburg* concluded its first deployment in March 2016, redeploying in September and concluding its mission on 14 November 2016.[^cite_note-210]

On 29 January 2016, the [Netherlands](21148.md) announced its intent on expanding its airstrike operations to [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-211]

In June 2016, [Omar Mateen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omar_Mateen), the attacker from the [Pulse nightclub shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulse_nightclub_shooting) pledged allegiance to the Islamic State and gave U.S. collaboration with Russia in airstrikes against I.S. as a reason for the attack.[^cite_note-212]

### Russian intervention

Main articles: [Russian military intervention in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_military_intervention_in_Syria) and [Russian involvement in the Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_involvement_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War)

Russian [Tu-95MS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tupolev_Tu-95) fires Kh-101 [cruise missiles](6590.md) at ISIL targets in Syria, September 2017

On 11 September 2015, a [Syrian military](27479.md) source made mention of Russian troops present in [Syria](7515849.md) to help the [Syrian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Syria) in its fight against [ISIL](9087364.md), as part of [Operation Rescue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Rescue_\(Syria\)?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214] On 17 September, Syrian warplanes carried out a wave of airstrikes in the ISIL-held city of [Raqqa](858965.md) with Russian weapons supplied by [Russian Armed Forces](25709.md).[^cite_note-215] On 20 November, [Russia](25391.md) claimed to have killed over 600 terrorists using cruise missiles in one mission.[^cite_note-216]

### Turkish intervention

Main articles: [Turkey–ISIL conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey%E2%80%93ISIL_conflict) and [Turkish involvement in the Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_involvement_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War)

ISIL is suspected of involvement in or responsibility for terrorist attacks in [Turkey](11125639.md) in [May 2013 in Reyhanlı](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Reyhanl%C4%B1_car_bombings) and [March 2014 on Turkish police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey%E2%80%93ISIL_conflict#March_2014_attack), [kidnapping 49 Turkish diplomats in June 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey%E2%80%93ISIL_conflict#June_2014:_ISIL_designated_terrorists,_kidnap_49_Turks), the [5 June 2015 Diyarbakır rally bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Diyarbak%C4%B1r_rally_bombing) and 20 July [2015 Suruç bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Suru%C3%A7_bombing) which killed 32 young activists. Until July 2015, the [Turkish government](3680197.md) [attacked ISIL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2014_Turkish_airstrike_in_Syria) only once, in January 2014. In September 2014 Turkey [joined a US-led coalition 'to fight ISIL'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_military_intervention_against_the_Islamic_State#International_coalitions_against_the_Islamic_State).

#### July 2015 special forces operation

On 23 July 2015, according to various Turkish news outlets, 60 elite [Special Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Forces_\(Turkish_Armed_Forces\)) (ÖKK) operatives reportedly infiltrated Elbeyli-Ayyase village, 9 kilometers from the [Syria-Turkey border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria-Turkey_border) in [Syria](7515849.md), and took it back from ISIL militants.[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-ulusalkanal-218][^cite_note-219] Turkish tanks shelled the village the same day of the ground operation.[^cite_note-220] The operation reportedly lasted over an hour and killed over 100 ISIL militants, according to reports.[^cite_note-ulusalkanal-218] The Turkish General Staff neither confirmed nor denied the special forces foray but did confirm shelling the village.[^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222]

The same day, [Turkey](11125639.md) [allowed the United States to use İncirlik and Diyarbakır air bases in southern Turkey for airstrikes on ISIL in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey%E2%80%93ISIL_conflict#July_23:_Turkish_air_bases_for_US_to_use_against_IS), and after [an alleged ISIL attack on a Turkish border outpost in Kilis Province killing one Turkish soldier, the Turkish army shelled ISIL militants in Syria, killing one militant and destroying several ISIL vehicles](#Elbeyli_incident).

On 24 July, an anonymous report appeared on a Turkish newspaper website stating that [the United States had agreed with Turkey on a 'partial no-fly zone' in northern Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey%E2%80%93ISIL_conflict#July_24:_'Turkish%E2%80%93American_zone_in_northern_Syria').

On 24 and 25 July the Turkish Air Force launched a military operation entitled '[Operation Martyr Yalçın](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Martyr_Yal%C3%A7%C4%B1n)' against both ISIL in [Syria](7515849.md) and the [Kurdistan Workers' Party](69680.md) (PKK) in [Iraq](7515928.md), deploying at least 70 [F-16](11642.md) [fighter jets](10929.md).

### Iranian intervention

Main article: [Iranian intervention in the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_intervention_in_the_Syrian_civil_war)

As the conflict intensified after the rise of IS in 2014 and the subsequent deterioration of the regime's control, Iran dramatically escalated its involvement.[^cite_note-223] By mid-2014, Iran began dispatching members of its elite [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](1285472.md) (IRGC), including its [Quds Force](4028355.md), along with other Iranian armed-forces personnel.[^cite_note-224] At the same time, Iran organized, trained and deployed a wide array of [Shiite](26961.md) militias: foreign volunteers, Iraqi militias that Tehran already supported, and locally raised Syrian pro-regime militias.[^cite_note-\:0-225]

At the height of its intervention from 2015-2018, estimates suggest that Iran had placed as many as 10,000 IRGC personnel and a substantial number of Iranian-led foreign militias on the ground in Syria.[^cite_note-\:0-225] Iranian forces backed by drones, ballistic-missiles, and air-power carried out hundreds of strikes against IS targets. By October 2018, Iranian drones alone had reportedly launched more than 700 strikes on Islamic State forces.[^cite_note-226]

By 2025 more than 2,100 Iranian soldiers had been killed in Syria.[^cite_note-227] The collapse of the regime's stability in late 2024 then significantly undermined Iran's influence in Syria.[^cite_note-228]

### Kurdish-led war

Main article: [Rojava–Islamist conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rojava%E2%80%93Islamist_conflict)

[Democratic Autonomous Administration of North and East Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Autonomous_Administration_of_North_and_East_Syria) is a major theater in the war against the Islamic State in Syria. During the Syrian Civil War, Kurdish forces led by the [People's Protection Units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Protection_Units) (YPG) took control of Northern Syria and launched campaigns to take control of the Islamist-controlled areas. The [Syrian Democratic Forces](48205248.md) went on to take substantial territory from the Islamic State and played a major role in the [liberation of Raqqa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raqqa_campaign_\(2016%E2%80%932017\)) and the [battle for Deir Ezzor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_ez-Zor_campaign_\(2017%E2%80%932019\)), ending Islamic State rule in Syria.

### Hezbollah intervention

Main article: [Hezbollah involvement in the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah_involvement_in_the_Syrian_civil_war)

The Lebanese Shiite militia and political-military group [Hezbollah](13919.md) intervened in Syria early in the civil war, initially as military advisers and fighters sent to support the regime of Assad, but over time their role grew both in scale and strategic importance.[^cite_note-\:1-229][^cite_note-\:2-230] Starting around 2012-2013, Hezbollah began deploying operatives to Syria, initially to assist in key regions like [Homs](77533.md), the [Qusayr area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qusayr_District), and around [Damascus](8914.md); including to defend [Shi'a](26961.md) holy sites and minority communities.[^cite_note-\:1-229][^cite_note-231]

Hezbollah's involvement was not only military: it helped organize, train, and lead many of the pro-regime militias later subsumed under broader entities like the [National Defence Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Defence_Forces) (NDF).[^cite_note-\:1-229][^cite_note-\:2-230]

Hezbollah's intervention in Syria effectively ended with the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md) in December 2024. The group withdrew its fighters, and clashes began between the new [Syrian transitional government](79584988.md) and Hezbollah along the [Lebanese-Syrian border](46232625.md).[^cite_note-232]

## Lebanon

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=War_against_the_Islamic_State\&action=edit\&section=). *(October 2025)* |
| --- | --- |

In 2014, during the Syrian Civil War, Lebanon [clashed against ISIL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Lebanon_clashes_\(2014\)) and other Jihadist insurgents present in the north of the country near the border with Syria.[^cite_note-233] In June 2015, [Hezbollah](13919.md) leader [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) claimed that ISIL and [Nusra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Nusra_Front) had taken a foothold in [Lebanon](17771.md) and that fierce battles were raging between them and Hezbollah, as well as each other.[^cite_note-234]

## Egypt

Main article: [Sinai insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_insurgency)

The Sinai insurgency began during the height of the [2011 Egyptian revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Egyptian_revolution) when Egyptian President [Hosni Mubarak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hosni_Mubarak) was overthrown and a [jihadist](7826589.md) group called the [Ansar Bait al-Maqdis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ansar_Bait_al-Maqdis) (ABM) launched attacks against [Egyptian security forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_security_forces) and civilians in the Sinai. Sometimes, the group conducted cross-border attacks against [Israel](9282173.md) as well.[^cite_note-AlJazeeraEgypt2026-235] After another Egyptian president, President [Mohamed Morsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Morsi), was [overthrown in 2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Egyptian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat), the ABM intensified its attacks against the Egyptian forces and governmental figures.[^cite_note-NCC2026-236][^cite_note-\:7-237]

### 2014 Islamic State rebranding

In November 2014, the ABM swore allegiance to the [Islamic State](9087364.md) and its then-leader, [Abu Bakr al-Baghdadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Bakr_al-Baghdadi).[^cite_note-AlJazeeraEgypt2026-235][^cite_note-\:5-238] After making the commitment, the group changed its name to [Islamic State – Sinai Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_%E2%80%93_Sinai_Province) (IS-SP) and gained recognition as an affiliate of the ISIS,[^cite_note-\:1-229] thus Egypt's involvement against the Islamic State became explicit.[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240][^cite_note-\:7-237] In 2015, the [United States](3434750.md) increased military aid to Egypt after lifting its suspension due to the 2013 coup. This included help with counterterrorism, sharing intelligence, and providing military equipment to fight Sinai-linked militants.[^cite_note-241] Israel also permitted Egypt to exceed the quantity of troops and heavy equipment Egypt could deploy in the Sinai than allowed under the [1979 Egypt–Israel peace treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Israel_peace_treaty).[^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] Israel also shared reconnaissance intelligence and sometimes performed airstrikes in the Sinai with Egyptian permission.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246]

### Sinai operations and clashes

Main articles: [Operation Martyr's Right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Martyr's_Right), [Metrojet Flight 9268](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metrojet_Flight_9268), [2017 Sinai mosque attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Sinai_mosque_attack), and [Comprehensive Operation – Sinai 2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Operation_%E2%80%93_Sinai_2018)

During this period, the Egyptian Armed Forces significantly expanded its operations by demolishing suspected terrorist-controlled buildings and underground smuggling [caves connecting Egypt and Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_smuggling_tunnels),[^cite_note-247] establishing buffer zones near the borders of [Gaza](12047.md),[^cite_note-\:3-248] implementing strict [curfews](297681.md) in the Sinai,[^cite_note-249] and turning to the [scorched earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorched_earth) strategy.[^cite_note-250] However, the Egyptian Security Forces were subsequently accused of [war crimes](48626.md), mainly by [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) (HRW), due to excessive force causing civilian casualties and widespread displacement.[^cite_note-\:3-248][^cite_note-251]



The aftermath of [Metrojet Flight 9268](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metrojet_Flight_9268)

Multiple intense clashes occurred, mainly in [January](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2015_Sinai_attacks) and [July](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/July_2015_Sinai_clashes) 2015, which resulted in heavy losses on both sides.[^cite_note-\:4-252] On 7 September 2015, Egypt launched [Operation Martyr's Right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Martyr's_Right), a six-phased long-term campaign aiming to "rooting out and killing 'terrorists'".[^cite_note-ArabCenterWashington112025-253][^cite_note-\:5-238] By late September 2015, during the second phase of the operation, the Egyptian military had impounded multiple weapons, ammunitions, vehicles, and communicating devices, with 535 militants dead with another 578 arrested.[^cite_note-254] Following clashes in early 2015, [Metrojet Flight 9268](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metrojet_Flight_9268), a Russian airliner flight flying from Egypt to [Russia](25391.md) with 224 people on board, disintegrated somewhere over northern Sinai by a bomb planted by the IS-SP, killing everyone on board.[^cite_note-\:4-252][^cite_note-255]

The insurgency had reached a deadlock by 2016 and 2017,[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257] with ongoing fighting leading to heavy casualties on both sides but no clear winner.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259]<sup>: 2 </sup> While Egyptian forces controlled major towns and carried out operations to contain the militants, the militants maintained a stronghold in rural and desert areas,[^cite_note-260] although some progress was made in the continued destruction of the underground [smuggling tunnels between Egypt and Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_smuggling_tunnels), increased enforcement of established buffer zones near the [Egypt–Gaza border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Gaza_border), and increased inspection of vehicles crossing the [Egypt–Libya border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Libya_border).[^cite_note-261] On 24 November 2017, a large group of the IS-SP militants coordinated [an attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Sinai_mosque_attack) on a mosque in northern Sinai, killing 311 people and injuring 128 more.[^cite_note-ArabCenterWashington112025-253] It is the deadliest terrorist attack in Egypt until today.[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264] Current Egyptian President [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](36703624.md) vowed for revenge and gave the Egyptian military a three-month deadline to launch an operation to stabilise the Sinai.[^cite_note-\:6-265]

On 9 February 2018, three months after the 2017 Sinai Mosque attack, one of the largest Egyptian operations in this conflict was launched by the [Egyptian Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_Armed_Forces).[^cite_note-\:6-265][^cite_note-ArabCenterWashington112025-253] Particularly in northern Sinai, the [Egyptian National Police](17957135.md) and the [Egyptian Military forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_forces) conducted multiple raids in buildings and intensified in the continued-demolition of buildings as well. Multiple airstikes were also performed. The [Egyptian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_Navy) also continued to secure the northern coast of the Sinai.[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267] A reported 464 militants were killed along with 7491 others being arrested.[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270]

In 2023, with the loss of around 3000 military men and with more than 12,000 injured, Egyptian President [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](36703624.md) declared victory over the militants.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-ArabCenterWashington112025-253] The high-level attacks conducted by the IS-SP were categorised as low-level attacks due to the significant military presence and continued military operations against the militants.[^cite_note-\:5-238][^cite_note-272]

### Direct international involvement

Main articles: [February 2015 Egyptian airstrikes in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2015_Egyptian_airstrikes_in_Libya) and [Egyptian intervention in Libya (2015–2020)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_intervention_in_Libya_\(2015%E2%80%932020\))

Egypt's first direct major international involvement against the Islamic State was in [Libya](17633.md) when, on 15 February 2015, some Islamic State militants [executed 21 Coptic Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21_Coptic_Christian_Martyrs_of_Libya), 20 of which were Egyptians.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] In retaliation, Egypt [launched airstrikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2015_Egyptian_airstrikes_in_Libya) against ISIS positions in Libya the next day using [F-16s](11642.md), which effectively [associated Egypt in the Second Libyan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_intervention_in_Libya_\(2015%E2%80%932020\)). The airstrikes had killed more than 60 ISIS militants and some civilians.[^cite_note-\:11-109][^cite_note-275]

## Iraq

Main articles: [War in Iraq (2013–2017)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Iraq_\(2013%E2%80%932017\)) and [Islamic State insurgency in Iraq (2017–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_insurgency_in_Iraq_\(2017%E2%80%93present\))

### US-led intervention

Main article: [US-led intervention in Iraq (2014–2021)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US-led_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%932021\))

For previous American interventions in Iraq, see [Gulf War (1990–91)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War), [Operation Desert Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Desert_Fox), and [Iraq War (2003–11)](5043324.md).



Iraqi soldiers present US Marine General [Joseph Dunford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Dunford) with a captured [ISIL flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jihadist_flag#ISIS_variant) during the intervention.



A 9th Iraqi army member, supported by Combined Joint Task Force-Operation Inherent Resolve, fires a machine gun from an M1 Abrams tank, during a battle with ISIS fighters near Al Tarab, Iraq, March 12, 2017.

After having started flying crewed aircraft over [Iraq](7515928.md) and sending some troops in June, in August 2014 the [US military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_military) began supplying Iraqi Kurdish [Peshmerga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshmerga) with weapons, dropping food for refugees fleeing from ISIL, and airstrikes against ISIL in [Iraq](7515928.md).

On 9 August, speaking about U.S. airstrikes in [Iraq](7515928.md), President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) said "this is going to be a long-term project."[^cite_note-276] Since then, nine countries[^cite_note-277] allied with the [US](3434750.md) have also executed airstrikes on ISIL in Iraq, and various countries have contributed military and humanitarian aid to [Iraqi government](11752991.md) and Iraqi Kurdish ground forces.

On 16–19 August, according to the U.S., Iraqi government forces and Kurdish Peshmerga, with the help of U.S. airstrikes, took back the [Mosul Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosul_Dam), the largest dam in [Iraq](7515928.md). (For further wins and losses in Iraq against ISIL, see [War in Iraq (2014–2017)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Iraq_\(2013%E2%80%932017\))). President Obama announced on 10 September 2014 that the number of airstrikes in Iraq would increase and that he had dispatched 500 more US troops there.[^cite_note-aje11sept-191]

On 10 September 2019, [US Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Air_Force) [F-35s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35) and [F-15E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-15E) Strike Eagles dropped bombs weighing 36,000 kg on an [Iraqi island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qanus_Island) "infested" by ISIS.[^cite_note-278]

#### Military aid

Further information: [US-led intervention in Iraq (2014–2021) § Military aid to the Kurds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US-led_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%932021\)#Military_aid_to_the_Kurds)

On 5 August 2014, [Zalmay Khalilzad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zalmay_Khalilzad), the former [US ambassador to Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_the_United_States_to_Iraq) and the [UN](31769.md), wrote in the *[Washington Post](102226.md)* that the [United States](3434750.md) is involved in "the direct supply of munitions to the [Kurds](17068.md) and, with Baghdad's agreement, the shipment of some [Foreign Military Sales](30857635.md) (FMS) program weapons to the Kurds."[^cite_note-BBC15Aug-279][^cite_note-280] The United States moved from indirectly supplying [Kurdistan](679693.md) with small arms through the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA) to directly giving them weapons such as [man-portable anti-tank systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man-portable_anti-tank_systems).[^cite_note-281]

In a coordinated effort led by the [United States](3434750.md), many allied countries including [NATO members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_states_of_NATO) and Middle Eastern partners have supplied or plan to supply Iraqi and/or Kurdish forces with heavy military equipment, small arms, ammunition, non-lethal military gear, and training support.

The [Building Partner Capacity (BPC) program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US-led_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%932021\)#Building_Partner_Capacity) is meant to help the [Iraqi government](11752991.md) to prepare forces for the counter-attack against ISIL and the regaining of its territory. According to the [US Department of Defense](7279897.md), by May 2015 a dozen countries had committed themselves to the BPC program: [Australia](4689264.md), [Belgium](3343.md), [Canada](5042916.md), [Denmark](76972.md), [France](5843419.md), [Germany](11867.md), [Italy](14532.md), [Netherlands](21148.md), [New Zealand](4913064.md), [Norway](21241.md), [Spain](26667.md), [United Kingdom](31717.md) and United States, and 6,500 Iraqi forces had been trained by BPC.

#### Humanitarian efforts

See also: [Genocide of Yazidis by the Islamic State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_of_Yazidis_by_the_Islamic_State) and [Sinjar massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinjar_massacre)



Bottled water containers are loaded on a U.S. Air Force C-17 for an [airdrop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airdrop) on 8 August 2014.

The [United States](3434750.md), the [United Kingdom](31717.md), and [Australia](4689264.md), supported by international partners, launched a large humanitarian effort to support refugees stranded in northern Iraq. This included air-dropping tens of thousands of meals and thousands of gallons of drinking water to [Yazidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazidi) refugees stranded in the [Sinjar Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinjar_Mountains) and threatened by advancing ISIL forces, between 7–14 August 2014, in what was later described as "the first mass air delivery of humanitarian cargo since the [outbreak of violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_East_Timorese_independence_referendum) in [East Timor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Timor) in 1999."[^cite_note-JTF633-282][^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286]

Thousands of [Yazidis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazidis) and other Iraqi civilians fled to the area following attacks on their villages and the town of [Sinjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinjar) throughout late July and early August 2014.

Several human rights and observer organizations in the region reported that those who fled to the mountains were subjected to starvation, and lacked clean drinking water and medical care for several months as ISIL [militants](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Militants) surrounded them. Hundreds of men, women, and children were abducted and killed.

In response to the immediate threat to the approximately 30,000 people trapped on the mountain, coalition aircraft commenced humanitarian aid drops. These air drops included basic supplies such as food, water, and shelter and were conducted at low flight levels by coalition transport aircraft under the threat of ISIL surface-to-air attacks.

In direct support of humanitarian aid drops, [CF-18s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CF-18) provided top cover for a [Royal Australian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Australian_Air_Force) (RAAF) [C-130 Hercules](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-130_Hercules) transport aircraft on 20 November, ensuring the transport crew was able to safely parachute supplies to waiting refugees below. [Canadian fighter jets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Forces) remained in close proximity to the transport aircraft to protect it from ISIL [surface-to-air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surface-to-air) threats or attacks.[^cite_note-287]

#### U.S. military operations

Main article: [Operation Inherent Resolve](44120613.md)

President Obama speaks about the "game plan" for dealing with the Islamic State.

Unlike their coalition partners, and unlike previous American combat operations, no name was initially given to the 2014 intervention against ISIL by the [U.S. government](195149.md).[^cite_note-288] The decision to keep the conflict nameless drew considerable media criticism.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] U.S. Service members remain ineligible for Campaign Medals and other service decorations due to the continuing ambiguous nature of the continuing U.S. involvement in Iraq.[^cite_note-294]

On 15 October 2014, the [United States Central Command](338482.md) announced that the U.S.-led air campaign against ISIL in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md) was henceforth designated as *[Operation Inherent Resolve](44120613.md)*.[^cite_note-CentComRel#20141018-295] The CENTCOM news release noted:

> "According to CENTCOM officials, the name INHERENT RESOLVE is intended to reflect the unwavering resolve and deep commitment of the U.S. and partner nations in the region and around the globe to eliminate the terrorist group ISIL and the threat they pose to Iraq, the region and the wider international community. It also symbolizes the willingness and dedication of coalition members to work closely with our friends in the region and apply all available dimensions of national power necessary—diplomatic, informational, military, economic—to degrade and ultimately destroy ISIL."[^cite_note-CentComRel#20141018-295]

##### U.S. airstrikes&#x20;

Main article: [US-led intervention in Iraq (2014–2021) § Timeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US-led_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%932021\)#Timeline)

[U.S. Navy](20518076.md) [Boeing F/A-18F Super Hornet](845012.md) bomb [ISIL](9087364.md) [artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M198_howitzer) targets on 8 August 2014.

In June 2014, U.S. forces had started undertaking reconnaissance missions over northern Iraq.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298]

On 7 August, President Obama gave a live address describing the worsening conditions in [Iraq](7515928.md) and that [the plight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Yazidis_by_the_Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant) of the [Yazidis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazidi) particular had convinced him that [U.S. military](32212.md) action was necessary to protect American lives, protect minority groups in [Iraq](7515928.md), and to stop a possible ISIL advance on [Erbil](24534207.md), the capital of the [Kurdish Autonomous Region](679693.md).[^cite_note-299] On 8 August, the [United States](3434750.md) started to bomb ISIL targets in Iraq.[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] By 10 August, assisted by these air attacks, Kurdish forces claimed to have recaptured the towns of Mahmour and Gweyr[^cite_note-302] from Islamic State control. Additional Iraqi airstrikes conducted in [Sinjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinjar) were reported to have killed 45 ISIL militants and injured an additional 60 militants.[^cite_note-303] On 11 August, a spokesperson for [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) said the airstrikes had slowed down ISIL's advance in northern Iraq, but were unlikely to degrade ISIL's capabilities or operations in other areas.[^cite_note-304] Between 8 and 13 August, U.S. airstrikes and Kurdish ground forces enabled 35,000 to 45,000 of Yazidi refugees to escape or be evacuated from the [Sinjar Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinjar_Mountains).[^cite_note-siege-305]

On 16 August, U.S. air power began a [close air campaign](600792.md) aimed at supporting the advance of Kurdish fighters moving toward the [Mosul Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosul_Dam). Kurdish sources commented that it was the "heaviest US bombing of militant positions since the start of air strikes".[^cite_note-BBC16Aug-306][^cite_note-BBC18Aug-307] President Obama on 17 August defended this usage of U.S. Forces as support of the Iraqi and Kurdish fight in general against ISIL—which indeed went beyond Obama's reasoning for launching airstrikes on 7 August.[^cite_note-308]



Locations where the United States has launched airstrikes against ISIS in Iraq (as of 16 September 2014).

On 8 September, the [Iraqi Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_Army), with close air support from the [U.S](3434750.md)., retook the key [Haditha Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haditha_Dam), and recaptured the town of [Barwanah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barwanah), killing 15 ISIL fighters.[^cite_note-VICE-309] ISIL responded with the public execution of [David Haines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Cawthorne_Haines).[^cite_note-Haines-310] By the end of September 2014, the United States had conducted 240 airstrikes in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md), as well as 1,300 tanker refueling missions, totaling 3,800 sorties by all types of aircraft. A tactical arrangement with Kurdish and Iraqi forces, and drone videos are being used to coordinate close air support without needing U.S. troops in ground combat.[^cite_note-311]

On 19 December 2014, US General James Terry announced that the number of US airstrikes on ISIL had increased to 1,361.[^cite_note-Pentagon_has_a_new_name_for_ISIS?-312]

On 25 December 2014, Hassan Saeed Al-Jabouri, the ISIL governor of [Mosul](200475.md), who was also known as Abu Taluut, was killed by a US-led Coalition airstrike in Mosul. It was also reported that the [US](3434750.md) planned to retake the city of Mosul in January 2015.[^cite_note-ISIL_governor_of_Mosul_is_killed-313]

On 15 January 2015, it was reported that over 16,000 airstrikes had been carried out by the Coalition. The [U.S. Air Force](32090.md) has carried out around 60 percent of all strikes. Among them, [F-16s](11642.md) performed 41 percent of all sorties, followed by the [F-15E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-15E) at 37 percent, then the [A-10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A-10) at 11 percent, the [B-1](4952.md) bomber at eight percent, and the [F-22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-22) at 3 percent. The remaining 40 percent has been carried out by the [US Navy](20518076.md) and allied nations.[^cite_note-314]

On 20 January 2015, the [SOHR](34029669.md) reported that al-Baghdadi, the leader of ISIL, had been wounded in an airstrike in [Al-Qa'im](1831738.md), an Iraqi border town held by ISIL, and as a result, withdrew to [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-ISIL_leader_wounded_in_airstrike-315]

On 21 January 2015, the US began coordinating airstrikes with a Kurdish launched offensive, to help them begin the planned operation to retake the city of [Mosul](200475.md).[^cite_note-Peshmerga_begins_assault_on_Mosul-316]

On 21 July 2015, it was reported that nearly 44,000 sorties have flown since August 2014.[^cite_note-317]

Throughout 2015, the vast majority of bombs and missiles launched by the [US](3434750.md) (approximately 22,000 of 23,000 total) were directed at targets in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md), according to the [Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_on_Foreign_Relations).[^cite_note-318]

In 2016, the [Pulse nightclub shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulse_nightclub_shooting) was carried out in retaliation for the U.S. Airstrikes in Iraq and Syria.[^cite_note-319]

In 2019 [U.S military carried out an airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_U.S._airstrike_in_Baghuz) in [Baghuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baghuz) town in [Syria](7515849.md) leading to death of 64 women and children, marking the largest civilian casualty incidents of the war against the [Islamic State](9087364.md). The incident was concealed by the U.S. military[^cite_note-NYT-320] and it was reported by [the New York Times](30680.md) for the first time on November 14, 2021.[^cite_note-321]

In June 2020, coalition aircraft destroyed three ISIL camps in northern Iraq.[^cite_note-322]

##### U.S. ground forces

In July, President Obama announced that due to the continuing violence in [Iraq](7515928.md) and the growing influence of non-state organizations, such as the [Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant](9087364.md), the [United States](3434750.md) would be elevating its security commitment in the region. Approximately 800 U.S. troops secured American installations like the [Embassy in Baghdad](4754335.md) and the Consulate in [Erbil](24534207.md) as well as taking control of strategic locations like the [Baghdad airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baghdad_International_Airport) in cooperation with Iraqi troops.[^cite_note-323]

U.S. forces also undertook a mission to "assess and to advise \[[Iraqi security forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_security_forces)] as they confront \[ISIL] and the complex security situation on the ground."[^cite_note-324] Reports from these American units about the capabilities of the Iraqi military have been consistently grim, viewing them as "compromised" by sectarian interests.[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327]

On 13 August 2014, the [U.S](3434750.md). deployed another 130 military advisers to Northern Iraq[^cite_note-328] and up to 20 [U.S. Marines](17349325.md) and special forces servicemen landed on [Mount Sinjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinjar_Mountains) from [V-22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V-22) aircraft to coordinate the evacuation of [Yazidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazidis) refugees joining British [SAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Air_Service) already in the area.[^cite_note-USUKTroops-329]

On 3 September 2014, Obama announced increase of U.S. forces in [Iraq](7515928.md) to 1,213.[^cite_note-330] On 10 September, Obama gave a speech reiterating that U.S. troops will not fight in combat, but about 500 more troops will be sent to Iraq to help train Iraqi forces.[^cite_note-aje11sept-191]

In early November 2014, Obama announced that he would be doubling the U.S. ground presence inside [Iraq](7515928.md) to around 3,000 men.[^cite_note-stripes.com-331] By early December 2014, the number of U.S. ground troops in Iraq had increased to 3,100.[^cite_note-Coalition_ground_troops_in_Iraq_increase-332]

On 9 December 2014, the [United States Senate Committee on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senate_Committee_on_Foreign_Relations) authorized U.S. military force against [ISIL](9087364.md). However, it limits military force to three years, requires the administration to report to [Congress](31756.md) every 60 days, and prohibits the deployment of U.S. combat troops, except in specific cases, such as those involving the rescue or protection of U.S. soldiers, or for intelligence operations.[^cite_note-333]



U.S. Army infantry from the [325th Infantry Regiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/325th_Infantry_Regiment_\(United_States\)) during the [Battle of Mosul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Mosul_\(2016%E2%80%9317\)), 7 March 2017

During the early morning hours of 14 December 2014, U.S. ground forces allegedly clashed with [ISIL](9087364.md) alongside the [Iraqi Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_Army) and Tribal Forces near the [Ain al-Assad Airbase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Asad_Airbase), west of [Anbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anbar_\(town\)), in an attempt to repel them from the base of which includes about 100 U.S. advisers in it, when ISIL attempted to overrun the base. According to a field commander of the [Iraqi Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_Army) in [Al Anbar Governorate](467036.md), said that "the U.S. force equipped with light and medium weapons, supported by [F-18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-18), was able to inflict casualties against fighters of ISIL organization, and forced them to retreat from the al-Dolab area, which lies 10 kilometers from Ain al-Assad base." Sheikh Mahmud Nimrawi, a prominent tribal leader in the region, added that "U.S. forces intervened because of ISIL started to come near the base, which they are stationed in so out of self-defense," he responded, welcoming the U.S. intervention, and saying "which I hope will not be the last."[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336] This was said to be the first encounter between the [United States](3434750.md) and the [Islamic State](9087364.md), [in four years](243750.md#April_6). However, this claim has been stated to be "false" by [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon).[^cite_note-337]

On 5 January 2015, [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) acknowledged that ISIL had been [ineffectively mortaring the base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Asad_Airbase#Al_Asad_Base_during_the_Intervention_against_the_Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant).[^cite_note-338]

In late February 2015, another 1,300 US soldiers were deployed to [Iraq](7515928.md), increasing the number of US ground troops in Iraq to 4,400.[^cite_note-1,300_more_US_soldiers_deployed_to_Iraq-339]

On 9 March 2020, the Pentagon released a statement claiming that two [American Marines](17349325.md) were killed on 8 March 2020 during an anti-ISIS operation in a mountainous area of north central Iraq.[^cite_note-340] Col. Myles B. Caggins III, a spokesman for the OIR coalition, later identified the Marines as Gunnery Sgt. Diego D. Pongo, 34, of [Simi Valley](18593507.md), [California](5407.md), and Capt. Moises A. Navas, 34, of [Germantown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germantown,_Maryland), [Maryland](18858.md), who were also MARSOC Raiders, and that they died during an operation which also claimed the lives of four ISIS fighters during an American-led operation which involved clearing an ISIS cave complex in the Makhmur Mountains, south of [Erbil](24534207.md).[^cite_note-341]

The coalition officially concluded its combat mission in Iraq on 9 December 2021, but U.S. troops remained in Iraq to advise, train, and assist [Iraqi security forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_security_forces) against the ongoing [ISIL insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_insurgency_in_Iraq_\(2017%E2%80%93present\)), including providing air support and military aid.[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-342][^cite_note-reuters.com2-343]

#### Australian airstrikes

Main article: [Operation Okra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Okra)

On 3 October 2014, [Prime Minister](24117.md) [Tony Abbott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Abbott) and the [Australian Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Australia) approved for [RAAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAAF) [Boeing F/A-18F Super Hornet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_F/A-18F_Super_Hornet) fighter bombers to begin airstrikes against Islamic State militants. Abbott said "It is in our national interest that we do so, it is in the interests of civilisation that we do so. It is in everyone's best interests that the murderous rage of the ISIL death cult be checked and rolled back and that's what we're determined to do."[^cite_note-344]

On 6 October, Air Chief Marshal [Mark Binskin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Binskin) announced two Super Hornets had conducted armed combat missions over [Iraq](7515928.md) although no armaments were expended. An Australian Air task Group KC-30A and an E-7A Wedgetail Airborne Early Warning and Control aircraft have also been flying in support to fighter bombers belonging to coalition forces. The KC-30A performs airborne refueling for coalition aircraft.[^cite_note-345] Binskin said "One of our Super Hornet packages on the first night ... had an identified target which it was tracking and that particular target moved into an urban area where the risks of conducting a strike on that target increased to a point where it exceeded our expectations of collateral damage, so they discontinued the attack at that point."[^cite_note-346]

On 9 October, Prime Minister [Tony Abbott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Abbott) confirmed that [RAAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Australian_Air_Force) Super Hornets had been involved in a "strike missions on an ISIL position in Iraq".[^cite_note-347] The aircraft dropped two bombs onto an isolated building which ISIL was using as a command and control center.[^cite_note-348]



[Peshmerga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshmerga) Fighters Enter Turkey Towards [Kobanî](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koban%C3%AE) after the [Kurdistan Regional Government](3949386.md) got Permission from [Turkey](11125639.md).

As of 17 October, the [Royal Australian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Australian_Air_Force) had conducted 43 combat sorties over Iraq.[^cite_note-349] Recent strikes had targeted equipment facilities, with "at least two" resulting in ISIL casualties after Australian aircraft had increased the number of missions flown to allow [U.S](3434750.md). and coalition forces to assist Kurdish fighters around [Kobanî](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koban%C3%AE), in northern Syria.[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351]

After more than 2 years of involvement in the coalition, [Australia](4689264.md) announced the end of its airstrikes in [Iraq](7515928.md), after informing Iraq and other allies.[^cite_note-352]

#### British airstrikes

Main article: [Operation Shader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Shader)



RAF [Tornado GR4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panavia_Tornado) over Iraq on an armed reconnaissance mission.

On 12 August 2014, the [United Kingdom](31717.md) deployed six [Tornado GR4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tornado_GR4) [strike aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_aircraft) to [RAF Akrotiri](432784.md) in [Cyprus](5593.md) to help coordinate its humanitarian aid airdrops in Northern Iraq.[^cite_note-353] On 16 August 2014, following the completion of humanitarian aid airdrops, the Tornado GR4s, along with an [RC-135 Rivet Joint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RC-135_Rivet_Joint) [signals intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signals_intelligence) aircraft, were re-tasked to provide aerial surveillance to coalition forces.[^cite_note-RAFRivetJoint-354]

In early September 2014, [British Prime Minister](24150.md) [David Cameron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Cameron) began voicing his support for British airstrikes against ISIL in [Iraq](7515928.md).[^cite_note-355] Weeks later, [Parliament](13964.md) was recalled and Members debated whether or not to authorise airstrikes. The seven-hour debate resulted in overwhelming support for airstrikes, with 524 votes in favour and 43 votes against.[^cite_note-356]

On 27 September 2014, the first armed sortie took place over [Iraq](7515928.md). A pair of Tornado GR4s left [Cyprus](5593.md) armed with laser-guided bombs, supported by a [Voyager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airbus_Voyager) aerial refueling tanker. Ultimately, the aircraft did not locate any targets requiring immediate air attack and so gathered intelligence for coalition forces instead.[^cite_note-firstUKsortie-357] The [Royal Air Force](25679.md) (RAF) conducted its first airstrike on 30 September 2014. A pair of Tornado GR4s engaged an ISIL heavy weapon position and an armed pickup truck using a laser-guided bomb and air-to-surface missile.[^cite_note-358]

On 3 October 2014, the [RAF](25679.md) deployed two additional Tornado aircraft to bring its deployed fleet up to eight aircraft.[^cite_note-359] During the same month, it was also confirmed that the [Royal Navy](26061.md) was involved in anti-ISIL operations in a support role, with air defence destroyer [HMS *Defender*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Defender_\(D36\)) providing escort to [U.S. Navy](20518076.md) aircraft carrier [USS *George H.W. Bush*](156573.md) as she launched aircraft into [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-HMSDefenderISIL-360] [Nick Clegg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Clegg), then [Deputy Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_the_United_Kingdom), also disclosed during an interview that there was a nuclear attack submarine armed with [Tomahawk cruise missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomahawk_\(missile_family\)) deployed to the region.[^cite_note-361]
