On 16 October 2014, the [Ministry of Defence](203793.md) announced it would deploy [MQ-9 Reaper](4769988.md) drones to assist with surveillance, although, [Defence Secretary](302332.md) [Michael Fallon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Fallon) stated that the drones could also conduct airstrikes if required.[^cite_note-362] The first Reaper drone strike occurred weeks later in [Bayji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayji), north of [Baghdad](4492.md), against a group of ISIL militants which had been laying improvised explosive devices.[^cite_note-UKStrikeList-363] As of September 2015, a year after operations first began, more than 330 ISIL fighters had been killed by British airstrikes in [Iraq](7515928.md), without any civilian casualties.[^cite_note-ISILDeaths-364][^cite_note-ISILDeaths2-365]

In addition to operations over [Iraq](7515928.md), the [United Kingdom](31717.md) had also intervened in [Syria](7515849.md) by 21 October 2014, making it the first Western country, other than the [United States](3434750.md), to do so.[^cite_note-UKinSyria-174] However, British aircraft were not permitted to carrying out airstrikes until [Parliament](13964.md) had voted to give its authorization. Despite this, the [Royal Air Force](25679.md) carried out a drone strike in Syria on 21 August 2015, against two UK-born ISIL fighters which had been plotting attacks against the United Kingdom. [Prime Minister](24150.md) [David Cameron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Cameron) insisted that it was a lawful act of self-defense.[^cite_note-\:9-203]



[ISIL](9087364.md)'s territory, in grey, at the time of its greatest territorial extent in May 2015

Since the authorization of airstrikes in [Iraq](7515928.md), Prime Minister David Cameron had made persistent calls for airstrikes in [Syria](7515849.md); however, he affirmed that no airstrikes would take place until after a vote in Parliament.[^cite_note-366] On 2 December 2015, following the [November 2015 Paris attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2015_Paris_attacks) and [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2249](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2249), David Cameron opened a ten-hour debate in Parliament on Syrian airstrikes, which ended with a final vote. 397 [MPs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_Parliament) voted in favour of airstrikes, whilst 223 voted against.[^cite_note-UKSyriaFacts-367] Airstrikes commenced two hours after the vote, taking place in eastern Syria against the ISIL-held Oman oilfield.[^cite_note-UKStrikeList-363] Defence Secretary Michael Fallon also subsequently announced that the UK's "strike force" based in [Cyprus](5593.md) would double, with the addition of six [Eurofighter Typhoons](167667.md) and two [Tornado GR4s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tornado_GR4).[^cite_note-UKSyriaFacts-367]

In addition to airstrikes, the [United Kingdom](31717.md) has also made significant contributions towards the coalition's [ISTAR](650971.md) capabilities. The [Royal Air Force](25679.md) has deployed [Sentinel R1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raytheon_Sentinel), [Sentry AEW1](10384.md), [RC-135W Rivet Joint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_RC-135) and [Shadow R1](9023483.md) aircraft to gather surveillance, in addition to Tornado GR4 and MQ-9 Reaper strike aircraft. In September 2015, the United Kingdom was responsible for a third of all coalition surveillance flights over [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md), with the Tornado GR4s [RAPTOR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAPTOR) reconnaissance pod accounting for 60% of the coalition's entire tactical reconnaissance in Iraq alone.[^cite_note-PodStats-368][^cite_note-OpShaderMOD-369]

In December 2016, the *[Telegraph](23797577.md)* reported that Secretary of State for Defence Sir Michael Fallon said "The [British Army](4887.md) have trained over 31,000 Iraqi and [Peshmerga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshmerga) who are taking the fight to Daesh"[^cite_note-telegraph1-370] It was also reported that the Royal Air Force is operating at its most intense for 25 years in a single theatre of operation which far outstripped the UK involvement in the [Iraq War](5043324.md) and the [War in Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Afghanistan_\(2001%E2%80%93present\)) (2001–2014), with RAF jets having dropped 11 times more bombs on [Syria](7515849.md) and [Iraq](7515928.md) in the preceding 12 months than they had in the busiest year of action in [Afghanistan](737.md) a decade previously.[^cite_note-telegraph1-370]

#### Canadian airstrikes

Main article: [Operation Impact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Impact)

[Canada](5042916.md) took part in airstrikes against ISIL from 2 November 2014 until 22 February 2016 when following the election of [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau) to Prime Minister withdrew its CF-18s fighter jets and ended all airstrikes in [Syria](7515849.md) and [Iraq](7515928.md).[^cite_note-371]

The Canadian contribution was code-named Operation Impact by the [Canadian Department of National Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_National_Defence_\(Canada\)).[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373] Canadian aircraft left for the [Middle East](19323.md) to join in airstrikes on 21 October 2014. In total, six [CF-18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonnell_Douglas_CF-18_Hornet) fighter jets, an [Airbus CC-150 Polaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airbus_CC-150_Polaris) air-to-air refueling tanker and two [CP-140 Aurora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_CP-140_Aurora) surveillance aircraft were sent, along with 700 military personnel.

Canadian [CF-18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonnell_Douglas_CF-18_Hornet) fighter jets completed their first operational flights departing from [Kuwait](7515890.md) on 31 October.[^cite_note-374] The first Canadian airstrikes began on 2 November.[^cite_note-375] [Canada](5042916.md) also flew an extra CF-18 to Kuwait to be used as a spare if the need arises, however a maximum of six are authorized to fly with the coalition missions.[^cite_note-376]

On 4 November 2014, [Royal Canadian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Canadian_Air_Force) CF-18s destroyed ISIL construction equipment using [GBU-12 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GBU-12_Paveway_II). The construction equipment was being used to divert the [Euphrates River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphrates_River) to deny villages water, and to flood roads, diverting traffic to areas with [IEDs](265112.md).[^cite_note-377]



A CF-18 during [Operation Impact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Impact)

On 12 November 2014, Canadian jets destroyed ISIL artillery just outside the Northern Iraqi town of [Baiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baiji,_Iraq).[^cite_note-Vice-Canada-378] Airstrikes continued throughout December and into January, 2015, totaling 28 strike missions.[^cite_note-torontostar1-379] It was then reported that Canadian special forces troops, which had been [highlighting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laser_designator) targets for airstrikes, had engaged in fighting after coming under attack.[^cite_note-torontostar1-379][^cite_note-globemaila-380]

On 19 January 2015, Canadian special operations forces came under ISIL attack for the first time in [Iraq](7515928.md), and returned sniper fire to "neutralize" the threat. [Canadians](19851291.md) are "enabling airstrikes from the ground," meaning they are actively finding targets for jets flying overhead.[^cite_note-381]

On 29 January 2015, Canadian special forces in [Iraq](7515928.md) came under fire from ISIL forces, causing the Canadian troops to return fire, killing some ISIL militants.[^cite_note-382] On 6 March, a Canadian soldier was killed in a [friendly fire](202484.md) incident by [Kurdish forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshmerga) while returning to an observation post.[^cite_note-\:12-102]

On 8 April 2015, two CF-18s carried out their first airstrike against ISIL in [Syria](7515849.md), hitting one of the group's garrisons.[^cite_note-383]

From 2 November 2014 to 13 May 2015 the Canadian armed forces struck 80 ISIL fighting positions, 19 ISIL vehicles, and 10 storage facilities.

On 21 October 2015, Canadian [Prime Minister-designate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister-designate) [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau) informed [U.S. President](24113.md) [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) that he intended to withdraw Canadian aircraft from operations over [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md) but increase training missions on the ground.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385]

On 8 February 2016, [Canadian Prime Minister](24135.md) [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau) announced that the CF-18s would be withdrawn from the bombing mission no later than 22 February 2016. However, the surveillance aircraft and air-to-air jet refueller would continue. In addition, the amount of training troops would triple.[^cite_note-386]

The [Canadian Government](616255.md) would extend the Operation until 31 March 2025.[^cite_note-387]

#### Dutch airstrikes

Main article: [Dutch war against ISIL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_war_against_ISIL)

On 24 September 2014, the [Dutch government](59761.md) announced its participation in "the military campaign" against ISIL which, as they claimed, had been started by the [United States](3434750.md), and sent six [F-16 fighter jets](11642.md) to [Iraq](7515928.md) to bomb ISIL. Their motivations to join this war: ISIL's advance in Iraq and [Syria](7515849.md), while displaying "unprecedented violence" and "perpetrating terrible crimes against population groups", formed "a direct threat for that region"; ISIL's advance in Iraq and Syria "causes instability at the borders of [Europe](9239.md)" which threatens "our own \[Dutch] safety". Figures requested by *[RTL Nieuws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTL_Nieuws)* in August 2015 showed that the [Netherlands](21148.md) was among the most active countries within the coalition, third behind only the [United States](3434750.md) and the [United Kingdom](31717.md).[^cite_note-388] In January 2016, the Netherlands extended their [bombings of ISIL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_war_against_ISIL) to Syrian territory. By the end of July 2016 the Dutch Air Task Force flew more than 2100 missions and carried out over 1800 air strikes.[^cite_note-389] At the end of the Dutch contribution to the Air Task Force, in December 2018, the [Royal Netherlands Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Air_Force) had flown over 3000 missions and conducted approximately 2100 air strikes.[^cite_note-390]



Combined [Air and Space Operations Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_and_Space_Operations_Center) (CAOC) at [Al Udeid Air Base](3830783.md), [Qatar](8238258.md), provides command and control of air power throughout Iraq and Syria.

#### French airstrikes

Main article: [Opération Chammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Op%C3%A9ration_Chammal)

On 19 September 2014, the [French Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Air_Force) used its [Rafale jets](33731380.md) to conduct airstrikes on IS targets in [Mosul](200475.md). The airstrikes were approved by [French President](24899.md) [François Hollande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande), which indicated that [France](5843419.md) was committed to fighting IS using air power alongside the [United States](3434750.md).[^cite_note-abcnews.go.com-391] Hollande mentioned that no ground troops would be used in the conflict. To conduct its airstrikes, France deployed 9 Rafale fighters to the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md), 6 [Dassault Mirage 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Mirage_2000) fighters to [Jordan](7515964.md), in addition to an [Atlantique 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breguet_Atlantic) maritime patrol aircraft, a [Boeing E-3 Sentry](10384.md) airborne early warning and control aircraft, and a [Boeing KC-135 Stratotanker](17441.md) aerial refueling tanker.[^cite_note-CDGWithdrawn-392]

On 23 February 2015, the [French Navy](383256.md) also deployed its [Task Force 473](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Force_d'action_navale) carrier strike group to the [Persian Gulf](24761.md) with the intent on conducting airstrikes from the aircraft carrier [*Charles de Gaulle*](639127.md). The *Charles de Gaulle* contributed 12 Rafale fighters, 9 [Dassault-Breguet Super Étendard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault-Breguet_Super_%C3%89tendard) strike aircraft, and 2 [E-2C Hawkeye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_Grumman_E-2_Hawkeye) airborne early warning and control aircraft. The task force also included the [French frigate *Chevalier Paul* (D621)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_frigate_Chevalier_Paul), a [Rubis-class submarine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubis-class_submarine), a [Durance-class tanker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durance-class_tanker), and the British frigate [HMS *Kent*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Kent_\(F78\)).[^cite_note-CDGDeployed-393] After eight weeks of operations, the task force left the Persian Gulf on its way to [India](14533.md), heralding the end of its contribution to [Operation Chammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Op%C3%A9ration_Chammal).[^cite_note-CDGWithdrawn-392]

On 5 November 2015, it was announced that the *Charles de Gaulle* would resume operations in [Syria](7515849.md) to fight IS.[^cite_note-394]

On 15 November 2015, after the [November 2015 Paris attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2015_Paris_attacks), the [French Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Air_and_Space_Force) launched its largest airstrike of the bombing campaign sending 12 planes, including 10 fighters, that dropped 20 bombs in training camps and ammunition facilities in [Raqqa](858965.md), the de facto capital of IS.[^cite_note-Airstrikes_against_ISIS-395]

#### Jordanian airstrikes

Main article: [Jordanian intervention in the Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_intervention_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War)

After the downed Jordanian pilot [Muath al-Kasasbeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muath_al-Kasasbeh) was executed by IS by being burned to death, [King Abdullah II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_II_of_Jordan) vowed revenge and temporarily took the lead in the bombing raids on IS during February 2015. On 8 February, [Jordan](7515964.md) claimed that during the course of 3 days, from 5–7 February, their airstrikes alone had killed 7,000 ISIL militants in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md), and also reportedly degraded 20% of the militant group's capability.[^cite_note-ISIL_mauled_by_Jordanian_airstrikes-396]

#### Moroccan airstrikes

In December 2014 [Morocco](19291.md) sent 4 F-16s to bomb ISIL positions, initially in the outskirts of [Baghdad](4492.md) and other undisclosed locations.[^cite_note-\:8-200] The planes operated under the command of the [UAE](69328.md) and suspended operations in February 2015.[^cite_note-397]

#### Turkish contributions

Main article: [Turkey-ISIL conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey-ISIL_conflict)

### Iranian intervention

Main articles: [Iranian intervention in Iraq (2014–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%93present\)) and [Iranian involvement in the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_involvement_in_the_Syrian_civil_war)

In mid-June 2014, according to American and British sources, [Iran](14653.md) sent [Qasem Soleimani](16805217.md), commanding general of the [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](1285472.md)' [Quds Force](4028355.md) (IRGC-QF), to [Iraq](7515928.md) to help the government organize against ISIL.[^cite_note-398] Later that month Iran started flying drones over Iraq,[^cite_note-NYT25-6-14-399] and by August, according to sources like [Reuters](18998750.md), Iranian soldiers were in Iraq fighting ISIL.[^cite_note-Reut3-8-14-400] One war correspondent suggested that Iran "joined the air war" against ISIL on 21 June.[^cite_note-401]

In July, according to the [International Institute for Strategic Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_for_Strategic_Studies), [Iran](14653.md) sent several [Su-25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukhoi_Su-25) aircraft to [Iraq](7515928.md),[^cite_note-bbc2Jul14-402] supported by Iranian/Iraqi ground crews trained in Iran.[^cite_note-bi1-11] In early August, those Su-25s began combat against ISIL, according to *[Business Insider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Insider)*.[^cite_note-bi1-11]

By September, according to *[Business Insider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Insider)*, [Iranian Quds Force](4028355.md) personnel were deployed to [Samarra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samarra), [Baghdad](4492.md), [Karbala](210957.md), and the abandoned [U.S. military](32212.md) post formerly known as [Camp Speicher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COB_Speicher).[^cite_note-bi1-11] At the end of November 2014, an Israeli website claimed to have seen Iranian [F-4 Phantom II](11759.md) jet-fighters bombing ISIL in eastern Iraq;[^cite_note-403] a claim the [U.S. army](32087.md) verified.[^cite_note-404]

In March and May 2015, American commentators indicated [Qasem Soleimani](16805217.md) was "leading Iraq's military strategy against ISIL".[^cite_note-\:13-45][^cite_note-405]

[Iran](14653.md) was mainly involved in the fight against Islamic State by supporting Shia militias from the [Popular Mobilization Units](45671374.md) both financially and militarily.[^cite_note-406]

### Hezbollah intervention

Already "for a long time" before June 2014, [Hezbollah](13919.md) had a presence in [Iraq](7515928.md) of advisers offering guidance to Shia fighters, according to a Hezbollah commander interviewed by *[The National](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_National_\(Abu_Dhabi\))*.[^cite_note-aljaz.31Jul-407]

In June 2014, [Hezbollah](13919.md) reportedly set up a dedicated command center in [Lebanon](17771.md) to monitor developments in [Iraq](7515928.md).[^cite_note-408] On 17 June, Hezbollah leader [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) said that the party was "ready to sacrifice martyrs in Iraq five times more than what we sacrificed in [Syria](7515849.md) in order to protect shrines."[^cite_note-409]

In July 2014, [Hezbollah](13919.md) sent more technical trainers and advisers to [Iraq](7515928.md), to monitor ISIL's movements, according to a Hezbollah commander.[^cite_note-aljaz.31Jul-407] Shortly thereafter, Hezbollah commander Ibrahim al-Hajj was reported killed in action near [Mosul](200475.md).[^cite_note-aljaz.31Jul-407]

An August [Reuters](18998750.md) story reported there were "dozens" of [Hezbollah](13919.md) "battle-hardened [veterans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veteran)" in [Iraq](7515928.md), while the *[Christian Science Monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Science_Monitor)* reported the party had deployed a 250-man unit "responsible for advising, training, and coordinating the Iraqi Shia militias."[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-reuir-411]

In February 2015, Nasrallah confirmed that he had sent troops to fight in [Iraq](7515928.md).[^cite_note-412]

## Libya

### Egyptian airstrikes

Main article: [February 2015 Egyptian airstrikes in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2015_Egyptian_airstrikes_in_Libya)

After [ISIL killed 21 Egyptian Coptic Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_kidnapping_and_beheading_of_Copts_in_Libya) in [Libya](17633.md),[^cite_note-aljaz-413] [Egypt](8087628.md) conducted airstrikes on ISIL targets in Libya on 16 February 2015, killing a total of 64 ISIL militants (50 in [Derna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derna,_Libya)).[^cite_note-\:11-109] Warplanes acting under orders from the "official" [Libyan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_National_Accord) also struck targets in Derna, reportedly in coordination with Egypt's airstrikes.[^cite_note-Libya_syncs_airstrikes_with_Egypt-414] A Libyan official stated that more joint airstrikes would follow.[^cite_note-Libya_syncs_airstrikes_with_Egypt-414]

### U.S. surveillance flights

Concern over ISIL activities in [Derna District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derna_District) in [Libya](17633.md) in December 2014 led to U.S. drones and electronic surveillance planes making "constant flights" from Italian bases, over the district of Derna.[^cite_note-US_begins_surveillance_flights_in_Libya-415]

### U.S. airstrikes

Main articles: [Battle of Sirte (2016)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Sirte_\(2016\)) and [American intervention in Libya (2015–2019)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_intervention_in_Libya_\(2015%E2%80%932019\))



An AV-8B Harrier assigned to the 22nd MEU aboard USS *Wasp* taking part in Operation Odyssey Lightning on 11 August 2016.

On 15 November 2015, the [United States](3434750.md) launched an airstrike in [Derna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derna,_Libya), [Libya](17633.md). Two U.S. F-15E fighter jets targeted senior ISIL leader [Abu Nabil al-Anbari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Nabil_al-Anbari) in the airstrike, who was the top ISIL commander in Libya.[^cite_note-usairstrike2-416][^cite_note-guardian-417] In January 2016, ISIL's Libyan faction confirmed Abu Nabil's death in a eulogy to him.[^cite_note-eulogy-418]

Administration officials are weighing a new campaign plan for [Libya](17633.md) that would deepen the United States' military and diplomatic involvement, on yet another front against ISIL. The [United States](3434750.md) and its allies are increasing reconnaissance flights and intelligence collecting there—and even preparing for possible airstrikes and raids, according to senior American officials. Special Operations forces have met with various Libyan groups over the past months to vet them for possible action against ISIL.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-419]

On 19 February 2016, US warplanes carried out an airstrike on multiple ISIL targets in Libya, hitting an Islamic State training camp and a senior extremist leader, the training camp was near [Sabratha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabratha), [Libya](17633.md), 60 people were present at the camp at the time of the strike, more than 40 people were killed with more wounded, some critically, On 14 February 2016, a [U.N](31769.md).-designated council presented a new 18-member Libyan cabinet in the Moroccan city of [Skhirat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skhirat), weeks after an earlier lineup was rejected. The internationally recognized parliament has to endorse the new unity cabinet. If approved, the new unity government could eventually seek international military intervention against Islamic State extremists who have taken advantage of the country's political vacuum since 2014.

On 1 August 2016, U.S. crewed and uncrewed aircraft carried out airstrikes on ISIL targets in [Libya](17633.md), responding to the [U.N](31769.md).-backed government's request to help [push the militants from their stronghold of Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Sirte_\(2016\)), in what U.S. officials described as the start of a sustained campaign against the extremist group in the city. President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) authorized the airstrikes after a recommendation by [U.S. Secretary of Defense](43998.md) [Ash Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ash_Carter); the strikes hit an ISIL tank and two vehicles that posed a threat to forces aligned with Libyan GNA ([Government of National Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_National_Accord)). This was the third U.S. air strike against Islamic State militants in Libya, but this time U.S. officials said it marked the start of a sustained air campaign rather than another isolated strike, U.S. airstrikes will continue to target ISIL in [Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirte) in order to enable the GNA to make a decisive, strategic advance. [U.S. AFRICOM](9347800.md) command is overseeing the US effort, which is known as Operation Odyssey Lightning, [AV-8B Harrier II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonnell_Douglas_AV-8B_Harrier_II) assigned to the [22nd MEU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/22nd_Marine_Expeditionary_Unit) flying off [USS *Wasp*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Wasp_\(LHD-1\)) conducted the airstrikes and uncrewed aircraft launched from undisclosed locations.[^cite_note-420][^cite_note-stars_and_stripes-421][^cite_note-ReferenceC-422][^cite_note-423][^cite_note-424][^cite_note-department_of_defence-425][^cite_note-426][^cite_note-military.com-427] Airstrikes continued, on 2 August, airstrikes hit a rocket launcher, an excavator and a pickup truck with a mounted recoilless rifle and on 3 August airstrikes struck a pickup truck with a mounted recoilless rifle; by 9 August the U.S. conducted 28 strikes against [ISIL in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant_in_Libya), with more than half of the strikes being conducted from uncrewed aircraft.[^cite_note-stars_and_stripes-421][^cite_note-department_of_defence-425] By 16 August, U.S. airstrikes hit an ISIL vehicle and 4 militant positions in Sirte, bringing the number of U.S. airstrikes in Libya to 48.[^cite_note-428] On 17 August, [U.S. Africa Command](9347800.md) officials announced on 16 August airstrikes on ISIL targets in Sirte struck 7 enemy fighting positions, 4 vehicle-borne bombs, 1 pickup truck with a mounted recoilless rifle, 12 enemy fighting positions and 1 command-and-control vehicle, bringing the total number of airstrikes in support of Operation Odyssey Lightning to 57.[^cite_note-429] On 22 August, *Stars and Stripes* reported that U.S. Marine [AH-1W SuperCobra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AH-1W_SuperCobra) helicopters participated in strikes against ISIL militants in Sirte on 20 and 21 August; a small detachment of US special forces in Sirte provided most of the targeting information for the airstrikes which were then relayed to U.S. forces through Libyan government troops.[^cite_note-430] On 31 August, *Stars and Stripes* reported that in the past month, the [U.S. military](32212.md) conducted 104 airstrikes against IS targets in Libya.[^cite_note-431]

On 22 September, *Stars and Stripes* reported that the pace of US airstrikes against IS militants in [Libya](17633.md) slowed in September as the number of insurgents holed up in a hard-to-target section of [Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirte) had shrunk, (the US conducted 50 airstrikes against IS targets, compared with 108 in August) with about 200 militants remaining.[^cite_note-stripes1-432] On 28 September, [Fox News](11121.md) reported that as of 26 September, [U.S. Marine Corps](17349325.md) Harrier jets and attack helicopters as well as drones conducted 175 airstrikes against ISIL in Libya, according to the U.S. military's Africa Command. According to a U.S. official the number of ISIL fighters in Sirte was estimated to be "under 100" and that "ISIS is only in three neighborhoods."[^cite_note-433]

On 3 October, *Stars and Stripes* reported that on 2 October the [US](3434750.md) conducted 20 airstrikes (bringing the total number of strikes to 201) in [Libya](17633.md): knocking out a command and control facility, nearly 70 IS fighting positions and several other sites in what was the heaviest day of bombing since the operation began, according to [U.S. Africa Command](9347800.md) data. The strikes were in support of an offensive by ground forces aligned with the [internationally backed Libyan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_National_Accord).[^cite_note-434] On 11 October, *Stars and Stripes* reported that U.S. warplanes conducted 51 airstrikes against ISIL targets in Libya, particularly in and around [Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirte), between 7 and 10 October, marking it as some of the heaviest bombing since the start of the Operation; bringing the total number of U.S. airstrikes in Libya to 261.[^cite_note-435] On 17 October, Fox News reported that US airstrikes against ISIS in Libya doubled in less than a month (bringing the number of airstrikes up to 324).[^cite_note-436] On 21 October 2016, *Stars and Stripes* reported that USS *San Antonio* deployed to the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md) as part of Operation Odyssey Lightning to replace USS *Wasp* that was carrying out operations against ISIS. *San Antonio* will carry UH-1Y Hueys and AH-1W Cobras from the 22nd MEU's Aviation Combat Unit, [VMM-264](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VMM-264); Marine Harrier fighters were part of the operation aboard *Wasp*, however *San Antonio* does not host fighter jets.[^cite_note-437]

On 4 November 2016, Fox News reported that the [U.S. military](32212.md) ended its bombing campaign against ISIS in [Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirte) after three months of round-the-clock airstrikes the U.S. military conducted a total of 367 airstrikes since 1 August 2016, according to officials, no American airstrikes took place since 31 October; units taking part in the operation received orders on 1 November from [AFRICOM](9347800.md) to end offensive and collective self-defence airstrikes. A senior defense official said the U.S. military would "continue to provide military support to the [GNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_National_Accord) ... ISIL-held territory in Sirte is down to a few hundred square meters. We'll continue to discuss with the GNA leadership what additional support they may need moving forward including air strikes."[^cite_note-438]

Sirte was liberated by GNA forces in early December; on 20 December 2016, *ABC news* reported that AFRICOM said that it carried out 495 airstrikes against militant vehicles and positions in the former IS stronghold of [Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirte),[^cite_note-439] Operation Odyssey Lightning concluded on 19 December, following an announcement from the Libyan government of the end of offensive military operations in Sirte.[^cite_note-440]

On 18 January 2017, *ABC News* reported that two USAF [B-2 bombers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_Grumman_B-2_Spirit) struck two ISIL camps 28 miles south of [Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirte). The airstrikes targeted between 80 and 100 ISIS fighters in multiple camps, an uncrewed aircraft also participated in the airstrikes. One official called the airstrikes "a huge success," with more than 80 ISIL fighters killed, one counterterrorism official told ABC News there were "zero survivors" at the camps. Many of the ISIS fighters in the camps had fled Sirte during the battle, according to another official; Pentagon press secretary Peter Cook said in a statement ISIS fighters had fled to the remote desert camps "in order to reorganize and they posed a security threat to [Libya](17633.md), the region, and U.S. national interests;" The militants were carrying weapons, wearing tactical vests and standing in formation. The airstrikes were authorised by President Obama and were carried out in coordination with GNA; they are considered to be an extension of Operation Odyssey Lightning.[^cite_note-441] *BBC News* reported that the B-2s flew a round-trip of around 34 hours from [Missouri](19571.md) and dropped around 100 bombs on their targets, US Defence Secretary Ash Carter said those targeted were "actively planning" attacks in [Europe](9239.md).[^cite_note-442] NBC News later reported that the number of ISIL fighters killed was revised upward to 90; a U.S. defense official said that "This was the largest remaining ISIS presence in Libya," and that "They have been largely marginalised but I am hesitant to say they've been completely eliminated in Libya."[^cite_note-443]

On 22 September 2017, the US military conducted 6 airstrikes with unmanned aircraft on an ISI: camp 150 miles southeast of Sirte, killing 17 militants and destroying three vehicles. CNN reported an AFRICOM statement that the strikes took place "In coordination with [Libya's Government of National Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_National_Accord) and aligned forces" and that "The camp was used by ISIS to move fighters in and out of the country; stockpile weapons and equipment; and to plot and conduct attacks". The strikes marked the first time airstrikes had been carried out in the country under the Donald Trump administration.[^cite_note-444]

### Other actions

The U.S. military has been closely monitoring ISIL movements in [Libya](17633.md), and small teams of U.S. military personnel moved in and out of the country over a period of months in early 2016. British, French, Italian and Jordanian special forces as well as the British RAF were also in Libya helping with aerial surveillance, mapping and intelligence gathering in several cities, including [Benghazi](204114.md) in the east and [Zintan](26063692.md) in the west, according to two Libyan military officials who were coordinating with them.[^cite_note-445][^cite_note-446][^cite_note-Middle_East_Eye-447] British and American special forces were also carrying out intelligence-gathering operations around [Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirte).[^cite_note-448]

Since the beginning of 2016, British Special forces have been escorting teams of MI6 agents to meet with Libyan officials and organise the supplying weapons and training to the [Libyan Army](35021571.md) and to militias fighting against ISIL.[^cite_note-\:10-449][^cite_note-450] On 27 February 2016, The Telegraph reported that British special forces had deployed alongside its U.S. counterparts in the city of [Misrata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misrata) to stop Islamist militants progress, their main role is to give tactical training to local militias and to build an army to fight ISIL.[^cite_note-The_Telegraph-451] In May 2016, it was reported that British special forces engaged in frontline combat against ISIL in [Libya](17633.md); in particular they destroyed two ISIL suicide vehicles that were targeting Libyan fighters. On 12 May, at the Shaddadah Bridge, 50 miles south of Misrata, the approach of a suicide vehicle sent Libyan forces fleeing in panic, British special forces intervened and destroyed the vehicle with a missile.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453] An estimated dozen U.S. special forces operated out of a base near Misrata and were in action near [Tripoli](31232.md).[^cite_note-454]

In a plan disclosed in late 2015, [Britain](31717.md) was to offer the [Libyan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_National_Accord) 1,000 troops as part of a 5,000-strong combined with [Italy](14532.md), to train and equip the Libyan forces rather than take part in frontline fighting.[^cite_note-The_Telegraph-451] In addition, British defence minister Michael Fallon announced that Britain is sending 20 troops from the [4th Infantry Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_Infantry_Brigade_and_Headquarters_North_East) to [Tunisia](30188.md) to help prevent Islamic State fighters from moving into the country from [Libya](17633.md).[^cite_note-455]

In June 2016, it was reported that ISIL militants were [retreating from Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Sirte_\(2016\)) and some fighters reportedly cutting off their beards and long hair to blend in with civilians as militia fighters allied to the unity government pushed into the city in tanks and armed trucks. The militias, mostly from [Misrata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misrata), are allied to and are the main fighting force for the [U.N](31769.md).-brokered unity government installed in [Tripoli](31232.md) the previous year.[^cite_note-456] On 11 June, the BBC reported that Libyan forces claimed they retook control of part of [Sirte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirte) after fierce fighting against ISIL militants.[^cite_note-457] In July 2016, [UN Secretary-General](162415.md) [Ban Ki-moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon) said ISIL fighters in [Libya](17633.md) were facing the "distinct possibility" of defeat in their last stronghold and are likely to scatter elsewhere in the country and the region;[^cite_note-military.com-427][^cite_note-458] At the beginning of 2016, ISIL was believed to have more than 5,000 fighters in Libya, by August 2016, estimates said there could be less than 1,000 left,[^cite_note-ReferenceC-422] and by 9 August, only 350 ISIL fighters remained alive in Sirte.[^cite_note-stars_and_stripes-421] U.S. and British special forces were involved in the battle for Sirte: U.S. troops were operating out of a joint operations center on the city's outskirts, their role was limited to supporting forces unity government forces, providing direct, on-the-ground support.[^cite_note-459] On 22 September, *Stars and Stripes* reported that Since the start of the battle, many ISIL members fled the city, "looking to hide among the population, relocate to other Libyan towns or attempting to leave Libya altogether."[^cite_note-stripes1-432]

## Afghanistan

Main articles: [War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](19666611.md) and [Islamic State–Taliban conflict](60786878.md)

The *BBC* reported that IS's announcement of the establishment of its [Afghanistan](737.md)/[Pakistan](23235.md)-based [Khorasan Province (IS-KP)](48029263.md) in January 2015, it was the first time that IS had officially spread outside the [Arab world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world). Within weeks, the group appeared in at least five provinces in [Afghanistan](737.md): [Helmand](457235.md), [Zabul](362239.md), [Farah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farah_Province), [Logar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logar_Province) and [Nangarhar](457263.md)-trying to establish pockets of territory from which to expand. In the first half of 2015, IS-KP managed to capture large parts of territory in eastern Nangarhar province. This became the de facto "capital" principally for two reasons: its proximity to the tribal areas of Pakistan, home of IS-KP's top leaders; and the presence of some people who follow a similar [Salafi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salafi_jihadism)/[Wahhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wahhabism) interpretation of [Islam](6037917.md) to IS. IS-KP is also trying to get a foothold in northern Afghanistan, where it aims to link up with Central Asian, Chechen and Chinese [Uighur](31783.md) militants; IS's numerical strength inside Afghanistan vary, ranging from 1,000 to 5,000.[^cite_note-auto2-460]

In February 2015, IS-KP deputy commander [Mullah Abdul Rauf Khadim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mullah_Abdul_Rauf_Khadim) was killed in a U.S. drone strike along with 5 others, his successor met the same fate a month later, and since then, the Islamic State has been absent from the southern [Afghanistan](737.md).[^cite_note-BBC31290147-461][^cite_note-462]

A report says that, according to a [Tehrik-i-Taliban Pakistan (TTP)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehrik-i-Taliban_Pakistan) spokesperson, in July 2015, a U.S. drone strike killed Shahidullah Shadid, a senior leader of IS-KP and 24 other militants, in [Nangarhar province](457263.md) in [Afghanistan](737.md).[^cite_note-463]

In January 2016, President Obama sent a directive to the Pentagon to make it easier for the military to get approval for strikes in [Afghanistan](737.md), targeting militias that have sworn allegiance to IS.[^cite_note-464] For 3 weeks in that month, the [United States military](32212.md) carried out at least a dozen operations, including commando raids and airstrikes, many of these raids and strikes taking place in the [Tora Bora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tora_Bora) region of [Nangarhar Province](457263.md). American commanders in Afghanistan said they believed that between 90 and 100 IS militants had been killed in these recent operations.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-419] On 1 February 2016, U.S. airstrikes in Nangarhar province eastern Afghanistan killed 29 IS fighters and struck the terrorist group's FM radio station.[^cite_note-465] On 21 February, it was reported that just over a week before, Afghan forces supported by U.S. airstrikes pushed IS militants out of their stronghold in Nangarhar province in a military operation that had killed a total 43 IS militants by 22 February.[^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467][^cite_note-468] On 6 March 2016, Afghanistan's president announced that the IS-KP had been defeated in the eastern parts of the country, Afghan forces claimed victory following the 21-day operation in 2 districts in Nangarhar province, claiming at least 200 militants killed.[^cite_note-469] following this operation, an official confirmed that IS-KP militants had moved into [Kunduz province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunduz_province) and into [Kunar province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunar_province).[^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471]

In early April 2016, it was reported that US and Afghan forces had killed 1,979 suspected militants, 736 others wounded and 965 detained between April 2015 and March 2016, IS militants have also been trying to flee into [Ghazni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghazni_Province) and [Nuristan province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuristan_province), whilst there has been a rise in defections from the group to the government and the [Taliban](30635.md).[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-US_department_of_defence-473] U.S. commanders in [Kabul](16826.md) have scaled back their threat assessment for IS-KP, since January, the U.S. and its allies launched between 70 and 80 airstrikes on IS militants in [Afghanistan](737.md).[^cite_note-474]

In late June 2016, IS militants attacked police checkpoints in the Kot area of [Nangarhar province](457263.md) and heavy fighting ensued, as many as 36 IS militants were killed in the assaults, at least a dozen Afghan security forces and civilians were killed, with another 18 wounded. The latest attacks indicate the group remains a potent threat to a government.[^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476]

On 8 July 2016, *The Guardian* reported that Prime minister David Cameron increased the number of British troops deployed to [Afghanistan](737.md) from 450 to 500, and that 21 of these additional troops would reinforce the counter-terrorism mission.[^cite_note-477]

On 23 July 2016, following the [Kabul bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/July_2016_Kabul_bombing), Afghan forces and U.S. special forces backed by U.S. airstrikes began an operation to retake parts on [Nangarhar province](457263.md) from IS-KP militants. Over 24 and 25 July whilst clearing areas of southern Nangarhar with Afghan special operations troops, 5 [U.S. special forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_special_operations_forces) troops were wounded by small arms fire or shrapnel, making it the first reported instance of U.S. troops being wounded in fighting IS in [Afghanistan](737.md). On 26 July, one of the most important leaders of IS in the region and one of the founders of the IS-KP, Saad Emarati, was killed along with 120 other suspected militants in [Kot District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kot_District), Afghan troops pushed into Kot District, meeting little resistance due to heavy air and artillery bombardment that forced IS fighters to flee into nearby mountain areas, Afghan forces found an already destroyed training camp. Overall, the operation reclaimed large and significant parts of eastern Afghanistan, forcing IS militants back into the mountains of southern Nangarhar with hundreds of IS militants killed; the estimated size of the IS-KP in January 2016 was around 3,000, but by July 2016 the number has been reduced to closely 1,000 to 1,500, with 70% of its fighters come from the TTP. In the operation, Afghan forces, backed by the US, killed an estimated 300 IS fighters.[^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479][^cite_note-480][^cite_note-481][^cite_note-482][^cite_note-the_guardian-483]

Between January and early August 2016, U.S. aircraft conducted nearly 140 airstrikes against IS targets in [Afghanistan](737.md), according to the U.S. military.[^cite_note-the_guardian-483]

On 4 October 2016, a US soldier from B Company, 2nd Battalion, [10th SFG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10th_Special_Forces_Group_\(United_States\)) was killed by a roadside bomb blast in [Achin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achin_District), [Nangarhar province](457263.md), he was on a patrol with Afghan forces during an operation against IS-KP militants.[^cite_note-484] This marked the first time a U.S. serviceman was killed in combat against IS militants in the country.[^cite_note-485]

On 24 December 2016, *Military.com* reported that Brigadier General [Charles Cleveland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_T._Cleveland) said that ISIL-KP's presence in the country has been pushed back from nearly a dozen districts to just two or three, the number of its members in [Afghanistan](737.md) had been reduced to about 1,000 from an estimated strength of between 1,500 and 3,000 members the previous year. Overall, U.S. troops in Afghanistan conducted more than 350 operations against the IS and al-Qaeda this year. In early December, General [John Nicholson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_W._Nicholson_Jr.), the international coalition's top military commander in Afghanistan, said U.S.-led counter-terrorism operations and [Afghan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Afghanistan) forces had killed 12 of the organization's top leaders in the country. U.S. officials have said IS fighters are primarily located in [Nangarhar](457263.md) and [Kunar](2159600.md) Province's.[^cite_note-486] *Military.com* reported that Nicholson estimated that his forces had killed about 500 ISIS fighters throughout 2016 (including the 12 most senior leaders), these losses accounted for about 25 to 30% of IS-KP's total number of fighters and reduced its foothold in the country from 9 districts to 3.[^cite_note-Military_Times-487]

In February 2017, the *Washington Post* reported that U.S. forces conducted more than 1,000 strikes in [Afghanistan](737.md) in 2016, including 267 against IS-K and 57 targeted al-Qaeda.[^cite_note-488] The *BBC* also reported that IS-KP has largely been eliminated from southern and western Afghanistan by the [Afghan Taliban](30635.md) and military operations conducted by Afghan and US/[NATO](21133.md) forces. Several hundred IS-KP fighters have been killed in clashes with the Afghan Taliban.[^cite_note-auto2-460]

In early April 2017, the *Washington Post* reported that Captain Bill Salvin, a spokesman for NATOs' mission to [Afghanistan](737.md) that Afghan and international forces have reduced IS-KP controlled territory in Afghanistan by two-thirds and killed around half of their fighters in the previous 2 years. Since the beginning of 2017, there have been 460 airstrikes against terrorists (with drone strikes alone killing more than 200 IS militants); he added that the affiliate has an estimated 600 to 800 fighters in two eastern [Afghan provinces](412343.md).[^cite_note-489]

The *Army Times* reported that in early March 2017, American and Afghan forces launched Operation Hamza to "flush" IS-KP from its stronghold in eastern [Afghanistan](737.md), engaging in regular ground battles.[^cite_note-490] *Stars and Stripes* reported that General Dawlat Waziri, spokesman for Afghanistan's Defense Ministry, said that for four weeks before the 13 April [Nangarhar](457263.md) airstrike (which was part of the operation), Afghan special forces unsuccessfully attempted to penetrate the area because of the difficult terrain and [improvised explosive device](265112.md) (IEDs) planted by IS-KP militants.[^cite_note-auto3-491] On 13 April, the [Nangarhar airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Nangarhar_airstrike) took place, *Stars and Stripes* reported that 94 IS-KP militants, including 4 commanders were killed by a [GBU-43/B MOAB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GBU-43/B_Massive_Ordnance_Air_Blast) bomb that was dropped on an IS tunnel complex in [Achin District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achin_District);[^cite_note-auto3-491] the *Huffington Post* reported that the bomb was dropped from a U.S.[Lockheed MC-130](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_MC-130).[^cite_note-492] In late April *Military Times* reported that Captain Bill Salvin said an estimated 400 to 700 fighters are active throughout Nangarhar and [Kunar](2159600.md) provinces.[^cite_note-Military_Times-487]

Sky News reported on 3 September 2018 that British special forces were also targeting IS-K in [Afghanistan](737.md) alongside US special forces.[^cite_note-493]

On 19 November 2019, *Stars and Stripes* reported that 243 IS fighters and nearly 400 family members surrendered to government forces in [Nangarhar Province](457263.md) during the previous two weeks. [Afghan President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Afghanistan) [Ashraf Ghani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashraf_Ghani) declared that "the result is Daesh's backbone was broken".[^cite_note-494]

On 26 August 2021 during the American evacuation from Afghanistan, a member of IS-K [detonated a bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabul_airport_attack) near a gate at [Kabul International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabul_International_Airport), killing at least 183 civilians and 13 US soldiers.[^cite_note-495] In the immediate aftermath of the attack, the US conducted a retaliatory strike on a vehicle in Nangarhar province which was believed to be carrying "high-profile ISIS targets" and "planners and facilitators."[^cite_note-496] Two days later, a US drone strike targeted what was believed to be a member of IS-K planning an attack, killing 10 civilians, including 7 children. The Pentagon later called the strike "an honest mistake" and announced that none of the military personnel involved would be subject to any disciplinary actions.[^cite_note-497]

### U.S. intervention in Cameroon

In October 2015, with the approval of the [Cameroonian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Cameroon), the U.S. military [deployed 300 personnel to Cameroon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_military_intervention_in_Cameroon), their primary missions will revolve around providing intelligence support to local forces as well as conducting reconnaissance flights.[^cite_note-498][^cite_note-499]

## The Philippines

Main articles: [Philippines and the Islamic State](55350198.md) and [Siege of Marawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Marawi)

On 1 September 2017, the US Secretary of Defence Mattis designated *Operation Pacific Eagle – Philippines (OPE-P)* as a contingency operation to support the [Philippine government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_the_Philippines) and the [Armed Forces of the Philippines](23448.md) in their efforts to isolate, degrade, and defeat the affiliates of ISIS (collectively referred to as ISIS-Philippines or ISIS-P) and other terrorist organisations in the [Philippines](23440.md).[^cite_note-500]

## Yemen

CNN reported that on 16 October 2017, US forces conducted airstrikes against two IS training camps located in [al Bayda Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Bayda_Governorate), [Yemen](350939.md), containing an estimated 50 fighters; a US defence official said that this is the first U.S. strike specifically targeting IS in Yemen,[^cite_note-501] the strike disrupted the group's attempts to train more fighters. CNN reported that on 23 October that two U.S. airstrikes in al Bayda Governorate, the first strike killed 7 IS terrorists travelling in pickup trucks, a second strike (5 miles west) killed a further 2 IS terrorists.[^cite_note-502] *Military Times* reported that on 25 October, two US airstrikes in al-Bayda Governorate killed 9 IS fighters, a CENTCOM statement said that "In the last ten days, U.S. forces have targeted and killed approximately 60 ISIS terrorists in Yemen." Maher Farrukh, an al-Qaida analyst for the Critical Threats Project at the American Enterprise Institute said that "[AQAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda_in_the_Arabian_Peninsula) and ISIS cooperate on a tactical level in central Yemen against al [Houthi](17240332.md)-[Saleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Abdullah_Saleh) forces, they often co-claim attacks and likely share some militants," and that "ISIS' continued presence in Yemen is likely sustained by its cooperation with AQAP, but it does not appear to be growing at this time."[^cite_note-503]

## Gaza Strip

### Before the separation of ISIS from al-Qaeda

Main article: [Battle of Rafah (2009)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Rafah_\(2009\))

The *[Islamic Emirate of Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Emirate_of_Rafah)* was a short-lived militant state established by the [Jund Ansar Allah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jund_Ansar_Allah), an Islamist group, in the town of [Rafah](584373.md) in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md). It was declared on 14 August 2009, by [Abdul Latif Moussa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdel_Latif_Moussa), also known as Abu Noor al-Maqdisi, during a sermon at the [Ibn Taymiyyah Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibn_Taymiyyah_Mosque?action=edit\&redlink=1). The emirate aimed to impose strict [Islamic law](28840.md) and was aligned with the ideology of [al-Qaeda](1921.md) and is believed to have been aided by ISIS.[^cite_note-504]

Jund Ansar Allah, translated as "Soldiers of the Followers of God," was founded by Abdul Latif Moussa in 2008. The group quickly garnered attention due to its extreme interpretation of Islam and its opposition to the ruling [Hamas government in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas_government_in_the_Gaza_Strip) (known locally as the *Palestinian Government in Gaza* or *Palestinian Authority in Gaza*). Tensions between Jund Ansar Allah and Hamas escalated when Moussa declared the establishment of the Islamic Emirate of Rafah, challenging the authority of Hamas.[^cite_note-505]

The proclamation of the Islamic Emirate of Rafah was seen as a direct threat to the Hamas government, which had been the de facto authority in [Gaza since 2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Gaza_\(2007\)) (after winning the [2006 Palestinian legislative election](38945340.md) but losing control of the West Bank Region in [subsequent conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatah%E2%80%93Hamas_conflict)). In response, Hamas launched a military operation against Jund Ansarf Allah. The confrontation culminated in a fierce battle at the Ibn Taymiyyah Mosque, where Abdul Latif Moussa and several of his followers were killed many of whom were [ISI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_of_Iraq) operatives. The emirate was dismantled, and Hamas reasserted its control over Rafah.[^cite_note-506]

### Palestinians in the Syrian civil war

See also: [Syrian civil war](30741795.md) and [Sectarianism and minorities in the Syrian civil war § Palestinians](34766543.md#Palestinians)

In 2012, Hamas publicly turned against the [Assad](364813.md) [government](20933383.md) and endorsed the [Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition) who were attempting to overthrow him.[^cite_note-Haniyeh_Cairo-507] In a speech in Cairo, when [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) was visiting from the Gaza Strip, he said, *"I salute all the nations of the [Arab Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring) and I salute the heroic people of Syria who are striving for freedom, democracy, and reform"*.[^cite_note-Haniyeh_Cairo-507] Government and opposition forces later both fought against ISIS in [a multi sided conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_armed_factions_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War#Opposing_forces).[^cite_note-508] This also put Hamas on a different side of the conflict to Iran, who Netanyahu also claims resemble to ISIS.[^cite_note-509][^cite_note-972_concocting-510]

The [Syrian civil war](30741795.md) and insurgency included [Aknaf Bait al-Maqdis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aknaf_Bait_al-Maqdis) (Arabic: أكناف بيت المقدس *"The environs of Jerusalem"*, Full name: كتائب أكناف بيت المقدس على أرض الشام *"Aknaf Beit al-Maqdis Brigades on the Land of the Levant"*),[^cite_note-Watan_-_ABM-511][^cite_note-Homepage_of_ABM-512] a Palestinian militant group in the [Yarmouk Camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yarmouk_Camp) in [Damascus](8914.md),[^cite_note-Watan_-_ABM-511] with ambiguous connections to Hamas.[^cite_note-Arabi21_ABM-513] The group fought against ISIS and against Assad government forces in the [Yarmouk Camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yarmouk_Camp).[^cite_note-Watan_-_ABM-511] Some sources say Hamas deny being connected to the group.[^cite_note-514]

Violence in [Yarmouk Camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yarmouk_Camp) first erupted in 2012.[^cite_note-UNWRA_Yarmouk_2015-515] In [2015, ISIS attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Yarmouk_Camp_\(2015\)) the [Palestinian refugee camp](400347.md) at [Yarmouk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yarmouk_Camp) on the outskirts of [Damascus](8914.md).[^cite_note-UNWRA_Yarmouk_2015-515] [ISIS attacked Yarmouk again in 2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Yarmouk_Camp_\(2018\)).[^cite_note-wafa_Yarmouk-516] Some PLO factions were involved in the fighting.[^cite_note-wafa_Yarmouk-516] The [al-Qaeda](1921.md) splinter group [Tahrir al-Sham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahrir_al-Sham) was involved.[^cite_note-AA_Tahrir_al-Sham-517][^cite_note-518][^cite_note-519] As of 2021, 160,000 were still displaced.[^cite_note-AJ_Yarmouk_2021-520][^cite_note-521]

The violence in the Yarmouk camp made the Assad government very unpopular in Palestine.[^cite_note-522][^cite_note-523][^cite_note-524]

### Hamas–ISIS conflict

See also: [Islamist anti-Hamas groups in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist_anti-Hamas_groups_in_the_Gaza_Strip) and [Salafi-jihadist insurgency in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salafi-jihadist_insurgency_in_the_Gaza_Strip)

Hamas have a history of violently suppressing [Islamic extremists in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist_anti-Hamas_groups_in_the_Gaza_Strip). They have particularly clashed with supporters of [Al-Qaeda](1921.md) and [ISIS](9087364.md), and groups who conducted attacks against [Palestinian Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Christians) or [other targets in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamism_in_the_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-525] In [2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_in_the_Palestinian_territories), Hamas security forces [eliminated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Rafah_\(2009\)) a small group of [Al-Qaeda](1921.md) sympathisers who established the [Islamic Emirate of Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Emirate_of_Rafah).[^cite_note-526][^cite_note-527][^cite_note-528][^cite_note-529][^cite_note-530][^cite_note-531][^cite_note-532]

ISIS arose in the [rubble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubble) of the [2003 United States-led invasion of Iraq](201936.md) and the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md) ([2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_in_Syria), [ongoing](2346678.md)), then later spread to the [Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula) and elsewhere.[^cite_note-BBC_mon-533] [Hamas in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas_government_in_the_Gaza_Strip) clashed directly with the Sinai Province, but Hamas were also connected to [groups on multiple sides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_armed_factions_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War) of the conflict with ISIS in Syria.

ISIS first discreetly issued threats to Hamas in 2015,[^cite_note-534] in the same video message they also threatened Hamas' [two rivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatah%E2%80%93Hamas_conflict) Israel and [Fatah](11104.md).[^cite_note-RÛDAW_2015-535]

#### Sinai Peninsula and Gaza Strip

See also: [Sinai insurgency § Gaza Strip spillover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_insurgency#Gaza_Strip_spillover)

In [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_in_Palestine), Hamas began a propaganda campaign to combat [extremist ideologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist_anti-Hamas_groups_in_the_Gaza_Strip) in the Gaza Strip, At the time they denied it was targeted at ISIS or any other specific group.[^cite_note-Jpost_2015-536] Mosques in the strip preached to promote a "centrist ideology".[^cite_note-Jpost_2015-536]

In 2017, [an ISIS suicide bomber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_Rafah_crossing_by_ISIS?action=edit\&redlink=1) at [Rafah Border Crossing](10475618.md) killed a [Hamas government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas_government_in_the_Gaza_Strip) [border guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_guard) (Nidal al-Jaafari, 28) and injured several others.[^cite_note-toi_suicide-537][^cite_note-AN_18_Aug_2017-538] Before anyone had claimed responsibility, Hamas described the bomber as an outlaw and *"a person of deviant ideology"*, Hamas' terminology for Islamic extremists.[^cite_note-AN_18_Aug_2017-538] Other factions also condemned the bomber.[^cite_note-paltimes_17_August_2017-539]

Hamas arrested dozens of [Salafi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salafi_movement) militants [in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist_anti-Hamas_groups_in_the_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-toi_war_video-540]

In early January [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_in_Palestine), Palestinians from the Gaza Strip who had joined [ISIS Sinai Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilayah_Sinai), captured and killed a man who they claimed was connected to the [Qassam Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qassam_Brigades).[^cite_note-AJ_nae5z-541] The killers made a video of the murder and released it as a "declaration of war" against Hamas.[^cite_note-542][^cite_note-toi_war_video-540] The speaker in the video is referred to as [Abu Kazem al-Maqdisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Kazem_al-Maqdisi?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-toi_war_video-540]

By 2023, the Egyptian branch of ISIS appeared to be completely dormant.[^cite_note-BBC_mon-533]

## Somalia

Further information: [Islamic State insurgency in Puntland](78785944.md), [Qandala campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qandala_campaign), and [Puntland counter-terrorism operations](78770768.md)

*Military.com* reported that on 3 November 2017, that a U.S. drone conducted two airstrikes against [Islamic State in Somalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_in_Somalia), at least six missiles were used which struck in Buqa, 37 miles north of [Qandala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qandala), AFRICOM said in a statement that "several terrorists" were killed and that the strikes were carried out in coordination with Somalia's government; the strikes marked first time that the US has conducted airstrikes against ISS terrorists in [Somalia](27358.md).[^cite_note-543] CNN reported that US drone aircraft conducted 5 strikes against [al-Shabaab](8726463.md) and ISS-linked militants between 9 and 12 November, killing 36 al-Shabaab and 4 ISIS terrorists. The [US](3434750.md) now estimates there are between 3,000 and 6,000 al-Shabaab fighters and less than 250 ISS operatives in Somalia.[^cite_note-544] The US conducted a three strikes in [al Bayda Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Bayda_Governorate) targeting ISIL in [Yemen](350939.md) between 10 and 12 November 2017, killing 5 suspected militants.[^cite_note-545]

&#x20; ISIL Presence

### ISIL

On 22 January 2015, U.S. Ambassador to Iraq Stuart Jones stated that the coalition airstrikes had degraded ISIL, including killing off half of their leaders in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-US_airstrikes_killed_over_6,000_ISIL_fighters-546]

In early February 2015, the Australian Defence Minister, [Kevin Andrews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Andrews_\(politician\)), stated that more than 6,000 ISIL fighters had been killed in coalition airstrikes since they began, and that over 800 square kilometres (310 mi<sup>2</sup>) had been recaptured; yet ISIL strength was estimated to have grown during this period to around 31,500 core fighters, including 3,000 fighters from Western nations.[^cite_note-547]

On 23 February 2015, U.S. General [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) stated that over 8,500 ISIL militants had been killed by Coalition airstrikes in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-548] In early March 2015, General Lloyd repeated this statement, saying that "ISIS has assumed a defensive crouch" in Iraq, and that "We are where we said we would be," in relation to the airstrikes.[^cite_note-549] This was in contrast to Jordan's claim that its airstrikes alone had killed 7,000 ISIL militants in Iraq and Syria over the course of 3 days, from 5 to 7 February 2015.[^cite_note-ISIL_mauled_by_Jordanian_airstrikes-396][^cite_note-Jordan-550]

In June 2015, U.S. Deputy Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) stated that over 10,000 ISIL fighters had been killed by Coalition airstrikes against the Islamic State.[^cite_note-551]

On 21 January 2016, France's defence minister [Jean-Yves Le Drian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Yves_Le_Drian) stated that over 22,000 ISIL fighters had been killed by Coalition airstrikes in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-552]

In August 2016, U.S. Army Lieutenant General Sean MacFarland told reporters at a news briefing "Although it's no measure of success and its difficult to confirm, we estimate that over the past 11 months we've killed about 25,000 enemy fighters. When you add that to the 20,000 estimated killed prior to our arrival, that's 45,000 enemies taken off the battlefield."[^cite_note-553]

In December 2016, a senior US military official told CNN that as many as 50,000 ISIL fighters have been killed since the war against the terror group began.[^cite_note-554]

In 2023, the US Central Command issued a statement announcing that it had killed IS leader Khalid Aydd Ahmed al-Jabouri. According to the statement, al-Jabouri had been involved in planning attacks in the Middle East and Europe. The statement also claimed that no civilians were killed in the strike.[^cite_note-555]

### Civilians

According to *Airwars*, a team of independent journalists, by August 2015, 450 civilians had been killed by the U.S.-led coalition air campaign against ISIL in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md) (of whom roughly 60% in Syria, 40% in Iraq). By that time, the [U.S](3434750.md).-led coalition officially acknowledged only two non-combatant deaths.[^cite_note-guar3-8-15-556] According to *Airwars*, by January 2016, "between 815 and 1,149 civilian non-combatants appear likely to have been killed in 135 incidents where there is fair reporting publicly available of an event, and where Coalition strikes were confirmed in the near vicinity on that date."[^cite_note-557]

According to *Airwars*, about 1000 civilians had been killed by the [U.S](3434750.md).-led coalition air campaign in March 2017 alone, gathering controversy and concern relating to the presidency of Donald Trump.[^cite_note-558]

According to *Airwars*, the air strikes and artillery of [U.S](3434750.md).-led coalition killed as many as 6,000 civilians in [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Syria](7515849.md) in 2017.[^cite_note-559][^cite_note-sms-560] According to *Airwars*, "In 2017 the war against ISIS \[Islamic State] moved into the most densely-populated urban centres controlled by the group, with dire results for civilians."[^cite_note-sms-560]

[Amnesty International](18947898.md) and monitoring group [Airwars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airwars) report said, more than 1,600 civilians were killed in [US](3434750.md)-led coalition include, United States, [Britain](31717.md) and [France](5843419.md), during the four-month airstrike campaign against ISIL group from the Syrian city of [Raqqa](858965.md) in 2017. The Coalition states have conducted 34,464 strikes against ISIL targets between August 2014 and end of March 2019, and killed at least 1,291 civilians.[^cite_note-561][^cite_note-562][^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564] According to a report published by [Syrian Network for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Network_for_Human_Rights) in 2024, 927 children lost their lives in coalition attacks in Syria.[^cite_note-565]

### Estimates by monitoring organizations

| Country | Estimated civilian casualties | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| United States | 8,000-13,000 | Airwars estimates \~13,000 deaths; U.S. Central Command acknowledges \~1,500.[^cite_note-airwars.org-566] |
| United Kingdom | \~1,000 | UK has confirmed only one death; Airwars estimates range much higher.[^cite_note-airwars.org-566] |
| France | 80-200 | No confirmed cases; Amnesty International and Airwars report substantial harm during Raqqa operations.[^cite_note-U.S.-led_Coalition_in_Iraq_&_Syria-567][^cite_note-568] |
| Netherlands | 77+ | Dutch strike on Hawija (2015) killed at least 70 civilians. Initially denied; later confirmed.[^cite_note-auto4-569][^cite_note-U.S.-led_Coalition_in_Iraq_&_Syria-567] Dutch airstrike on residential building in Mosul killed at least seven civilians, per investigative reporting.[^cite_note-570] |
| Turkey | \~55 | Airwars estimates 17 incidents where civilians were killed in strikes by the Turkish military, primarily in Syria, between 2019 and 2022.[^cite_note-571][^cite_note-572] |
| Canada | 37+ | Canadian strike on Mosul (January 2015) allegedly killed 6-27 civilians.[^cite_note-\:15-573][^cite_note-\:16-574][^cite_note-\:17-575][^cite_note-\:18-576][^cite_note-\:19-577] Canadian airstrike on East Mosul (November 2015) reportedly killed 10 workers at a dairy plant.[^cite_note-\:15-573][^cite_note-\:16-574][^cite_note-\:17-575][^cite_note-\:18-576] |
| Australia | 30-70 | One confirmed death; others reported but unacknowledged, including an Australian airstrike on Mosul that reportedly killed 18 civilians.[^cite_note-U.S.-led_Coalition_in_Iraq_&_Syria-567][^cite_note-578][^cite_note-theguardian.com-579] |
| Denmark | 5-10 | No official investigations; small number of reported incidents tracked by Airwars.[^cite_note-airwars.org-566] |

#### Official responses from coalition governments

- **United States**: Has officially acknowledged about 1,500 civilian deaths, far below Airwars' estimate of 8,000-13,000.

  [^cite_note-airwars.org-566]

  &#x20;While some internal Pentagon investigations have occurred, Amnesty International and Human Rights Watch have criticized the processes as opaque and insufficient.

  [^cite_note-580]

- **United Kingdom**: Officially acknowledged only one civilian death, despite independent estimates suggesting far higher numbers.

  [^cite_note-airwars.org-566]

  &#x20;Repeated calls from Airwars, Amnesty International, and members of Parliament for independent investigations have not resulted in comprehensive reviews.

  [^cite_note-581]

- **France**: Has not acknowledged any civilian casualties. The French Ministry of Armed Forces rarely comments on individual strikes. Amnesty International reports significant civilian harm during operations in Raqqa but no official French investigations.

  [^cite_note-U.S.-led_Coalition_in_Iraq_&_Syria-567]

  [^cite_note-582]

- **Netherlands**: Initially denied any casualties in the 2015 Hawija strike, later confirming the deaths of at least 70 civilians after investigative journalism revealed the incident.

  [^cite_note-auto4-569]

  &#x20;Critics described the government's internal investigation as limited and lacking transparency.

  [^cite_note-583]

  [^cite_note-584]

- **Turkey:** The Turkish Defense Ministry denies that there had been any civilian casualties.

  [^cite_note-585]

  &#x20;Amnesty International reports that some Turkish airstrikes in northeast Syria targeted civilian infrastructure, including schools and markets.

  [^cite_note-586]

- **Canada:** Officially maintains a record of zero civilian deaths, despite independent monitors identifying credible incidents involving at least 37 fatalities.

  [^cite_note-587]

  [^cite_note-588]

  [^cite_note-\:17-575]

  [^cite_note-\:18-576]

  [^cite_note-\:19-577]

  &#x20;Airwars and Amnesty International criticized Canada's flawed investigative process that ignores ground-level evidence, arguing that the Canadian government has avoided transparency and accountability.

  [^cite_note-\:17-575]

  [^cite_note-589]

- **Australia**: Acknowledged one civilian death in 2021, years after the incident, but has not publicly investigated other alleged cases.

  [^cite_note-U.S.-led_Coalition_in_Iraq_&_Syria-567]

  [^cite_note-theguardian.com-579]

- **Denmark**: No civilian deaths have been officially acknowledged, and Danish authorities have not publicly investigated reports of civilian harm linked to their strikes.

  [^cite_note-airwars.org-566]

## Labeling

See also: [Labelling suicide attacks as terrorism](18835454.md#terrorism)

On 1 February 2015, Iraq's Foreign Minister [Ibrahim al-Jaafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahim_al-Jaafari) stated that the war on ISIL was effectively "[World War III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_III)", due to ISIL's proclamation of a [worldwide caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worldwide_caliphate), its aims to conquer the world, and its success in spreading the conflict to multiple countries outside of the [Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levant) region.[^cite_note-Iraq's_Prime_Minister_equates_the_War_on_ISIL_to_World_War_III-590] Speaking of ISIL's destruction of pre-Islamic sites in the region, Syria's head of antiquities, Maamoun Abdul Karim, stated that "this is the entire world's battle".[^cite_note-591] In June 2015, U.S. Deputy Secretary of State Antony Blinken said that ISIL "stands for nothing and depends on people who will fall for anything."[^cite_note-592]
